an unparseable reply is quoted by its first `QUOTED_OUTPUT_CHARS` characters only, the other number that doc states | `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `adapters/model_command.py`, `adapters/conversation_cli.py` | `test_a_flooding_role_is_stopped_at_the_cap_before_it_finishes`, `test_the_control_output_under_the_cap_is_parsed`, `test_output_that_is_not_utf8_is_a_malformed_output_not_a_crash`, `test_a_large_request_does_not_deadlock_a_child_that_answers_first`, `test_a_child_that_never_finishes_is_a_transient_failure_and_is_reaped`, `test_a_timed_out_role_takes_its_grandchild_with_it`, `test_a_flooding_wrapper_takes_its_grandchild_with_it_too`, `test_a_flooding_export_is_a_record_defect_stopped_at_the_cap`, `test_a_flooding_discovery_is_stopped_at_the_cap_too`, `test_an_export_that_is_not_utf8_is_a_record_defect_not_a_crash`, `test_a_hanging_command_is_unavailable_and_the_child_is_reaped`, `test_a_failing_export_names_the_exit_code_and_quotes_a_bounded_stderr_tail`, `test_a_failing_export_that_said_nothing_says_so`, `test_a_failing_export_with_only_blank_stderr_is_quoted_from_stdout`, `test_a_role_that_exits_non_zero_is_a_transient_failure_quoting_a_bounded_stderr_tail`, `test_a_role_that_exits_non_zero_in_silence_says_so`, `test_the_stderr_quote_bound_in_the_privacy_doc_is_the_shared_constant`, `test_the_diagnostic_quotes_only_the_documented_head_of_the_output`, `test_the_output_quote_bound_in_the_privacy_doc_is_the_shared_constant`, `test_the_control_export_under_the_cap_is_normalized` |
| A role leaves nothing running in its group when it exits | `run_bounded` in `adapters/bounded_process.py`, asked by the model runner (`end_group_on_exit`), ends the role's process group once the role has exited, whatever its exit code, before the call returns and the role's scratch is removed. The bounded reader killed that group only while the role itself still ran (at the deadline, the cap or a stop signal), so a role that exited on its own left whatever it had started. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, with no sandbox and under `sandbox-exec`, the same in all four): a background `sleep`, the same under `nohup`, a subshell ignoring the hang-up, termination and interrupt signals, a double fork, a loop of 200 background jobs and the background job of a role that then exited 3 each ran on after the call returned, and a job that made the role's scratch again after its removal and wrote there left it in the state tree, 7 cases of 7; after, 0 of 7. The exit is waited for without reaping the role (`_exited_unreaped`: `waitid` with `WNOWAIT` where there is one, on Linux and on macOS from Python 3.13, else a kqueue), so while its group is signalled the role's id still names that group and cannot have passed to another process; the role is reaped only after. While SIGCHLD is ignored in the run, the kernel reaps the role itself as it exits, so no role is started then (row "No child is started while its exit could not be read"), and a role SIGCHLD came to be ignored for as it ran is not signalled (`_children_reaped_by_the_kernel`; found by this row's first audit: on 3.11, where macOS waits with a kqueue, that exit was taken for one not yet reaped and the freed id was signalled, in 5 calls of 5, measured with the signal only recorded; after, in none on either Python). A run started with SIGCHLD ignored (a parent can leave it so through `exec`, and Python does not reset it) puts it back to the default before anything else (`_put_sigchld_back` in `cli.py`): macOS does not reap the children of a process started so, yet `getsignal` reports the ignore, so with that check alone the group was left as if the kernel had reaped the role, and what the role started ran on in 5 calls of 5 (measured on both Pythons, though the role was an unreaped zombie each time and its group was ended without that check); Linux keeps the ignore through `exec` and then reaps each child as it exits (read from its kernel/signal.c, not run here), as macOS does with SIGCHLD ignored in the run, where each child's exit then reads as 0 (measured on both Pythons, before the row "No child is started while its exit could not be read": a role's answer that exited 3 taken, 5 of 5, and an export that exited 1 taken, 5 of 5). The commands such a run starts then start with SIGCHLD at the default too (measured). The group is signalled in rounds (`_end_group`) until nothing in it runs, since one signal misses a child forked as it arrives (measured on macOS, both Pythons: with one signal, a shell starting a background job, a double fork or a loop of 200 as the role exited left a process running in the group in 19 to 37 runs of 40; with the rounds, in none of 40); the rounds end when macOS reports that the group can no longer be signalled, or after `GROUP_END_SECONDS` (1 s). On Linux there is one round (`ONE_ROUND_ENDS_A_GROUP`), since Linux gives a group's signal sent during a fork to the new child too, and reports a signal to an exited member not yet reaped as sent, so rounds there could not tell when nothing was left (read from its kernel/fork.c and kernel/signal.c, not measured here; CI runs this module on Linux). The kill at the deadline and the cap goes in the same rounds. Controls: a role that closes its output and works on is not cut short, and one still running at the deadline after closing it times out as before; a call that leaves nothing signals its group once; where the exit cannot be waited for without reaping, nothing is signalled, as before, so a role still at work is not ended; and the conversation command, the operator's own tool and not a role, keeps a helper it starts. 31 mutants (the runner not asking, or every command asked; the group not ended, or ended after the role is reaped; a wait that cannot tell taken as an exit; the exit not waited for; one round everywhere, on macOS, or nowhere; an error not ending the rounds; their bound ignored, or changed; a group that cannot be signalled not reported; `_kill` without its fallback, or in one round; `waitid` reaping, a failed `waitid` taken as an exit, or `waitid` not waiting; a kqueue's no-such-process event, or the same error raised, not taken as an exit, any error event taken as one, or its timeout taken as one; the kqueue preferred to `waitid`; no way to wait taken as an exit; an ignored SIGCHLD not checked, every SIGCHLD taken as ignored, or the default taken as ignored; and the run not putting SIGCHLD back, putting it back to ignored, doing so only off the main thread, or a call of `main` doing so too) are each caught on both by the module of this row, apart from three: one round nowhere is the code as written on macOS, and on Linux fails the module's check of the rule (not run here); and `waitid` reaping, or not waiting, is caught on 3.13 only, since 3.11 on macOS has no `waitid` and waits with the kqueue. Not covered by this row: a process that moved to a process group of its own (`setsid`, `setpgid`), which the signal to the role's group does not reach, so it runs on after the call (measured, with no sandbox and under `sandbox-exec`), with what that lets it do to the scratch as the row "A role cannot keep its scratch by changing its modes or flags" says; a platform with neither `waitid` nor a kqueue, where the group is left as before; a caller of `main` or of `run_bounded` in a process started with SIGCHLD ignored, or one that ignores it in the run, where no role is started (the row "No child is started while its exit could not be read" says what that still leaves); SIGCHLD set to be ignored from C code in the run after it started, which `getsignal` does not see (measured: it still reported the default while the kernel reaped each child), so the kqueue's exit is taken for one not yet reaped as before this row's audit; and a process the kernel cannot end at once (in an uninterruptible wait), which is signalled for `GROUP_END_SECONDS` and left dying | `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `adapters/model_command.py`, `docs/PRIVACY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `cli.py` | `test_what_a_role_leaves_running_in_its_group_is_ended_when_it_exits`, `test_a_child_forked_as_the_group_is_signalled_is_reached_too`, `test_a_role_that_exits_non_zero_leaves_nothing_running_either`, `test_a_confined_role_leaves_nothing_running_and_no_scratch`, `test_a_role_that_keeps_working_after_closing_its_output_is_not_cut_short`, `test_a_role_still_running_at_the_deadline_after_closing_its_output_times_out`, `test_the_group_is_ended_before_the_role_is_reaped`, `test_a_call_that_leaves_nothing_signals_its_group_once`, `test_where_the_exit_cannot_be_waited_for_unreaped_nothing_is_signalled`, `test_the_limit_a_process_that_moved_to_a_group_of_its_own_runs_on`, `test_the_control_a_conversation_command_keeps_what_it_starts`, `test_an_exited_child_is_reported_and_left_unreaped`, `test_a_running_child_is_waited_for_until_the_timeout`, `test_the_form_follows_what_the_platform_has`, `test_a_failed_wait_is_not_taken_for_an_exit`, `test_a_kqueue_error_other_than_no_such_process_is_not_taken_for_an_exit`, `test_the_group_is_signalled_again_until_nothing_in_it_runs`, `test_on_linux_one_round_is_all`, `test_the_rounds_are_bounded`, `test_a_group_that_cannot_be_signalled_falls_back_to_the_child`, `test_an_ignore_taken_up_while_a_role_runs_leaves_its_group_unsignalled`, `test_with_sigchld_ignored_no_exit_is_taken_for_one_not_yet_reaped`, `test_a_run_started_with_sigchld_ignored_puts_it_back_to_the_default`, `test_a_run_started_with_sigchld_ignored_leaves_nothing_running_in_the_role_group`, `test_a_run_started_with_sigchld_ignored_reads_a_role_exit_as_it_is`, `test_a_call_of_main_keeps_the_sigchld_its_caller_left` |
| No child is started while its exit could not be read | `run_bounded` in `adapters/bounded_process.py` starts nothing while SIGCHLD is ignored in the process and raises `ExitUnreadable`, an `OSError`, so the model runner reports the role as a command that cannot be started (a transient failure, so the conversation is deferred) and the conversation source reports its command as one it cannot execute (the source is unavailable). With SIGCHLD ignored the kernel reaps each child itself as it exits, and Python's `subprocess` then reads every exit as 0. Measured before, with SIGCHLD ignored in the run (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both): a role's answer that exited 3 was taken, 5 calls of 5; an export that printed and exited 1 was taken as the export, 5 of 5; the kill at the output cap went to an id the kernel had already freed, 5 of 5 (measured with the signal refused unless `ps` still showed the id as this process's exited child); and a background job a role started ran on after the call, 5 of 5. A process started with SIGCHLD ignored that calls the library rather than `asi` (which puts SIGCHLD back as it starts: row "A role leaves nothing running in its group when it exits") read every exit right on macOS, where an ignore inherited through `exec` does not make the kernel reap, but left a role's background job running, 5 of 5, since the ignore is reported all the same. After, on both Pythons, each of these is refused before anything starts, 5 of 5, and nothing is left running; a daily run called as a library with SIGCHLD ignored in the run, which read `status=ok` with 5 conversations scored, now defers all 5, each with the role named as a command that cannot be started. Control: with SIGCHLD at its default a role's exit 3 and an export's exit 1 are reported as before, and an answer is taken. 6 mutants (the refusal removed, made after the child is started, made for model roles only, made at the default instead, made an exception other than an `OSError`, or saying nothing of SIGCHLD) are each caught on both by the module of this row. Not covered by this row: an ignore taken up while a child runs, which only a signal handler or C code could set while the call waits: its exit is not taken for one not yet reaped (row "A role leaves nothing running in its group when it exits"), but a kill at the deadline or the cap may reach an id already freed, and its exit reads as 0; SIGCHLD set to be ignored from C code, which `getsignal` does not see (measured: it still reported the default, and every case above went as before this row, on both Pythons), so the child is started and its exit reads as 0; `doctor` called as a library with SIGCHLD ignored in the run, which still says ready (measured), though `daily` then defers every conversation as above; and the one child started elsewhere, the isolation probe (`adapters/sandbox.py`), whose verdict is read from what it prints, not from its exit status (the detail it records quotes that status, which then reads 0) | `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `adapters/model_command.py`, `adapters/conversation_cli.py`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` | `test_with_sigchld_ignored_a_role_is_not_started_and_its_answer_is_not_taken`, `test_with_sigchld_ignored_an_export_is_not_started_nor_taken`, `test_with_sigchld_ignored_a_role_past_its_cap_signals_no_id`, `test_with_sigchld_ignored_nothing_a_role_would_start_runs`, `test_run_bounded_refuses_before_it_starts_anything`, `test_a_library_call_started_with_sigchld_ignored_is_refused`, `test_the_control_with_sigchld_at_its_default_a_child_runs_as_before` |
| A quoted failure must not persist the credential it is about | The stderr tail a role or the conversation CLI leaves, the head of a non-JSON model reply and the conversation command line itself are redacted with the transcript's shapes before they are cut to the documented length and become a `reason`, so a key that straddles the cut is masked whole rather than sliced into an unrecognised fragment; harmless text is cut exactly as before. The same masking covers a model role's argv wherever it is persisted (its command id in scorecards, candidates and receipts; its dump in the config report) and the confinement probe's quoted output in the receipt's isolation record, while the argv that actually runs is untouched. A value a model put inside its JSON is quoted the same way: `quoted_value` in `domain/redaction.py` (the `repr`, redacted whole, then cut at `QUOTED_VALUE_CHARS` with the number of characters not quoted) is the one way a refusal quotes a scorecard's schema or `conversation_id`, a criterion id the rubric does not know or that was scored twice, a judgement's schema or preferred label, a proposal's schema, the improver's `target_skill_id` or the frontmatter name its replacement would introduce, and the two the scorecard itself keeps: a verdict string the rubric does not know (`Verdict.parse` in `domain/rubric.py`, whose message becomes a note and a `downgrade_reason`) and an evidence `element_id` that does not resolve in the conversation, which the downgrade reason names bounded, with its `position` bounded too (a JSON integer has no bound; five refs at 4300-digit positions once made one reason of 12,985 characters), and the scorecard stores nowhere else, since a citation that does not resolve is the model's claim rather than evidence and the stored refs feed the improver prompt; a value that fits is quoted whole. Measured before: these were interpolated with `!r`, so a scorer putting four megabytes, or a pasted key, into `conversation_id` would have put it in the quarantine reason, the receipt, the daily text summary and the scheduler log | `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `adapters/conversation_cli.py`, `adapters/model_command.py`, `adapters/sandbox.py`, `domain/redaction.py`, `domain/rubric.py`, `domain/scoring.py`, `domain/validation.py`, `domain/candidate.py`, `app/pipeline.py` | `test_a_failing_export_that_prints_a_credential_has_it_redacted_in_the_reason`, `test_a_credential_in_the_configured_command_line_is_redacted_from_the_reason`, `test_a_role_that_exits_printing_a_credential_has_it_redacted_in_the_reason`, `test_the_quoted_head_is_redacted_before_it_lands_in_a_quarantine_record`, `test_quoting_redacts_the_whole_capture_before_it_cuts`, `test_a_credential_in_a_role_argv_is_redacted_from_its_command_id_and_dump`, `test_a_probe_that_prints_a_credential_has_it_redacted_from_the_detail`, `test_a_short_value_is_its_repr`, `test_a_long_value_is_cut_and_the_cut_is_counted`, `test_a_credential_is_redacted_whole_before_the_cut`, `test_a_refusal_quotes_the_models_value_bounded_and_redacted`, `test_the_control_a_short_value_is_quoted_whole`, `test_an_unresolved_element_id_is_quoted_bounded_redacted_and_not_stored`, `test_an_unresolved_position_is_quoted_bounded_like_its_element_id`, `test_the_control_a_small_unresolved_position_is_named_whole`, `test_exactly_as_many_unresolved_refs_as_are_shown_says_no_more`, `test_a_ref_that_resolves_is_stored_beside_one_that_does_not`, `test_an_unknown_verdict_is_quoted_bounded_and_redacted`, `test_a_huge_or_secret_preferred_label_is_quoted_bounded_and_redacted`, `test_the_control_a_short_wrong_label_is_quoted_whole`, `test_a_huge_schema_value_is_cut_and_a_secret_one_redacted`, `test_the_control_a_short_wrong_schema_is_quoted_whole` |

## State holds verbatim private transcripts

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A permissive umask must not decide the mode | Every directory is created `0700` and every artifact written `0600`, set explicitly; the test runs under `umask(0)` and proves the umask really was permissive | `app/state.py`, `jsonio.py` | `test_the_state_root_is_0700_even_under_a_permissive_umask`, `test_every_declared_subdirectory_is_0700`, `test_the_readme_is_0600`, `test_the_umask_really_was_permissive_during_the_test`, `test_the_state_tree_declares_a_sandbox_subdirectory_and_it_is_owner_only` |
| Raw exports and derived artifacts are equally sensitive | Raw exports, conversations, scorecards, failures, receipts, backups and the lock are all owner-only | `app/state.py`, `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `app/lock.py` | `test_a_raw_export_is_0600`, `test_conversations_scorecards_failures_and_receipts_are_all_0600`, `test_a_backup_directory_is_0700`, `test_the_run_lock_is_0600_even_when_it_already_existed_wider` |
| The run lock must be a file this tool owns | The lock leaf is opened without following a symlink and judged with `fstat` before its mode is set or a byte is read or written, so a planted fifo or device is a named refusal rather than a hung run, a wrong `chmod`, or a write to somewhere else | `app/lock.py` | `test_a_symlinked_lock_leaf_is_refused_without_touching_its_target`, `test_a_fifo_at_the_lock_path_is_refused_without_being_waited_on` |
| The atomic writer must not widen a mode | The mode is applied to the temp file before the rename, so no window exists at a wider mode and nothing is left behind | `jsonio.py` | `test_the_atomic_writer_defaults_to_0600_and_not_to_the_umask`, `test_the_atomic_writer_honours_an_explicit_mode`, `test_write_json_also_lands_0600`, `test_no_temp_file_is_left_behind_at_a_wider_mode` |
| A state directory that is really a symlink redirects the whole private tree | The root and every mandatory subdirectory must be a real directory, measured with `lstat` *before* anything follows, chmods, creates through, reads through or writes through it; a dynamically named directory such as a backup stamp is proven to be a real direct child of an already-verified mandatory directory; `doctor` reports the layout as a problem and repairs nothing, so the outside target's mode and contents are unchanged; an aliased ancestor of the root is refused on the lazy subdirectory access every non-`daily` command takes, exactly as on `ensure()`; the ancestor walk follows the path as written, so a `..` after a link is not collapsed away before the link is looked at, and an absent component does not end the walk while a later `..` can step back over it onto a link; configuration resolves only the ancestors of `[run].state_dir` and keeps its last component as written, so a link planted at the configured state directory reaches the store as a link, `doctor` reports it before anything (its sandbox scratch root included) touches the tree, and `daily` refuses | `app/state.py`, `cli.py`, `config.py` | `test_a_state_root_that_is_a_symlink_is_refused_before_anything_is_created`, `test_a_lazy_subdirectory_access_refuses_a_symlinked_ancestor_too`, `test_a_dotted_path_through_a_symlinked_ancestor_is_refused_not_collapsed`, `test_a_dotted_path_that_steps_back_over_an_absent_component_still_meets_the_link`, `test_the_control_a_dotted_path_through_real_directories_is_accepted`, `test_a_symlinked_state_root_receives_no_layout_and_no_artifact`, `test_a_mandatory_subdirectory_that_is_a_symlink_is_refused`, `test_a_write_through_a_symlinked_mandatory_subdirectory_is_refused`, `test_a_repair_walk_does_not_chmod_through_a_redirected_layout`, `test_a_mandatory_state_directory_that_is_a_regular_file_is_refused`, `test_a_precreated_backup_directory_symlink_is_refused`, `test_a_backup_directory_is_created_as_a_direct_child_of_backups`, `test_a_backup_manifest_is_not_read_through_a_symlinked_backup_directory`, `test_the_control_shows_a_healthy_layout_reports_no_problems`, `test_an_arbitrary_leaf_symlink_is_still_skipped_rather_than_refused`, `test_doctor_reports_a_symlinked_state_subdirectory_without_chmodding_it`, `test_a_configured_state_root_that_is_a_symlink_is_refused_through_the_cli` |
| An already-wide tree is the case a fresh-run check misses | `ensure()` creates, repairs and then verifies; a repair that cannot take effect is a refusal rather than a warning, and a symlink is skipped rather than followed; what the walk skipped is named (`foreign_entries()`, `doctor`'s `left_alone` and a `WARN`) rather than silently passed, and a leaf that is not a regular file is neither counted as a record nor opened for long (a fifo is refused, not waited on) | `app/state.py`, `cli.py` | `test_a_pre_existing_world_readable_tree_is_detected`, `test_it_is_repaired_and_the_repair_is_reported`, `test_ensure_repairs_a_pre_existing_tree_rather_than_refusing_it`, `test_a_repair_that_cannot_take_effect_is_a_refusal_not_a_warning`, `test_the_control_shows_a_healthy_tree_does_not_refuse`, `test_a_symlink_in_the_tree_is_skipped_rather_than_followed`, `test_every_entry_the_mode_walk_skips_is_named_rather_than_silently_passed`, `test_a_symlinked_leaf_is_not_counted_as_a_record`, `test_a_fifo_in_the_tree_is_named_left_alone_and_never_opened_for_long`, `test_doctor_names_a_link_inside_a_confined_tree_as_a_warning_not_a_problem` |

## Promotion is the only write, and it is deliberately awkward

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Applying needs a human, provably | Four independent interlocks: an approval token, a typed acknowledgement, a matching base hash, and no automation marker | `domain/approval.py`, `app/promote.py` | `test_08_apply_without_a_token_is_refused`, `test_09_apply_without_the_acknowledgement_is_refused`, `test_10_apply_with_a_wrong_token_is_refused`, `test_11_apply_under_the_automation_marker_is_refused`, `test_12_the_skill_file_is_still_untouched_after_four_refusals` |
| An approval must not survive a change to what it approved | The token is a **deterministic review interlock, not a security boundary**: it is derived from the candidate id, the base hash and the patch hash with no secret, so anyone who can run this tool can derive one -- what it buys is that an approval cannot outlive an edit to the base or the patch | `domain/approval.py` | `test_the_token_is_bound_to_the_candidate_the_base_and_the_patch`, `test_changing_the_base_hash_invalidates_the_token`, `test_changing_the_patch_invalidates_the_token` |
| A token must not be recoverable from the artifacts | The token is never written into the receipt | `app/promote.py` | `test_the_token_is_not_written_into_the_receipt` |
| A stale base must be refused | If the file changed since the candidate was built, apply refuses even with a valid token | `app/promote.py` | `test_18_a_stale_base_is_refused_even_with_a_valid_token`, `test_apply_refuses_a_live_base_that_moved_under_the_candidate` |
| A stale base must be named as a stale base, on review as on apply | `review` checks the record against itself first, then the live file, then the record against the live file, in the same order as `apply`, so an edited skill is reported as `BaseHashMismatch` with the re-propose remedy rather than as a tampered record with advice to delete it; a record that fails its own base-independent recomputation is still named tampered first even when the file also moved | `app/promote.py` | `test_review_names_an_edited_skill_as_a_base_mismatch_not_as_tampering`, `test_review_still_names_a_tampered_record_first_when_the_skill_also_moved` |
| A token must bind the bytes that were reviewed, not a field beside them | Every derived field is recomputed before review, apply and validation: the patch hash from the normalized `new_text`, the approval challenge from the id and both hashes, the diff and its counts from the *live* base, the canonical target identity, and the status against the recorded gate. The token itself is derived from the recomputed hash. Editing only `patch.new_text` used to leave the token valid and put unreviewed text into a skill file | `domain/integrity.py`, `domain/candidate.py`, `app/promote.py`, `app/validate.py` | `test_04_apply_with_the_original_token_is_refused`, `test_05_the_skill_file_is_byte_identical_after_the_refusal`, `test_editing_new_text_alone_is_detected_although_every_other_field_is_intact`, `test_the_token_of_a_record_whose_text_changed_is_not_the_reviewed_token`, `test_an_edited_unified_diff_is_detected_against_the_live_base`, `test_a_rewritten_approval_challenge_is_detected`, `test_a_skill_id_that_disagrees_with_the_path_is_reported`, `test_a_validated_status_over_a_failing_gate_is_reported`, `test_08_control_the_untampered_record_reviews_to_the_same_token`, `test_09_control_apply_still_succeeds_on_the_record_that_was_reviewed` |
| A validation must be evidence about the failures the candidate names | Every target outcome is bound to a candidate failure ref by the whole `(rubric_id, conversation_id, criterion_id)` triple: the record's claim set must equal the candidate's own `rubric_failures`, no target outcome may name a triple the candidate does not claim, and an outcome must be attributed to a rubric that really grades its criterion. Substituting a rubric that is live with a matching version and source hash used to pass unnoticed, because the binding was only `conversation::criterion` | `domain/validation.py`, `domain/integrity.py` | `test_substituting_a_live_matching_rubric_on_the_outcome_is_refused`, `test_substituting_the_rubric_on_the_claim_set_too_is_still_refused`, `test_an_outcome_whose_named_rubric_cannot_grade_it_is_refused`, `test_two_rubrics_may_grade_the_same_criterion_id_without_colliding`, `test_a_target_outcome_re_attributed_to_the_other_live_rubric_is_refused`, `test_re_attributing_the_claim_set_as_well_is_still_refused`, `test_a_clean_record_reports_no_mismatch_with_the_live_rubrics` |
| A promotion-eligible candidate must carry exact target coverage | Adding or omitting a claimed failure invalidates review and apply, and a claimed target with no replay outcome fails the gate where it is produced rather than becoming a nominally validated record that refuses itself at review time | `domain/integrity.py`, `app/validate.py` | `test_an_added_candidate_failure_ref_invalidates_the_record`, `test_an_omitted_candidate_failure_ref_invalidates_the_record`, `test_adding_a_ref_to_both_sides_without_an_outcome_fails_the_recomputed_gate`, `test_an_added_candidate_failure_ref_is_refused`, `test_an_omitted_candidate_failure_ref_is_refused`, `test_a_failed_target_replay_fails_the_gate_and_writes_a_readable_record`, `test_a_gate_failed_record_with_an_unreplayed_claim_is_honest_not_tampered` |
| A count no verifier can recompute is not evidence | `evidence_model_calls` is derived exactly from the persisted outcomes and judgements and must equal that derivation; calls that were attempted and produced nothing usable are reported as a note instead of inflating the field. The previous lower-bound check accepted any overcount | `domain/validation.py`, `app/validate.py` | `test_one_extra_call_is_refused`, `test_one_missing_call_is_refused`, `test_the_call_count_is_the_evidence_count_and_the_attempts_are_a_note`, `test_one_extra_evidence_model_call_is_refused`, `test_a_second_criterion_on_a_scored_pair_costs_no_further_call`, `test_each_distinct_conversation_rubric_pair_costs_two_calls` |
| A record the writer emits must parse under its own parser | Rubric bindings and judgements are derived from the outcomes that persisted rather than from every attempted case, so a failed or partial replay still writes a record the strict parser accepts, and no judgement exists without the outcome it is about | `app/validate.py`, `domain/validation.py` | `test_a_partial_multi_rubric_replay_binds_only_the_rubric_that_produced_evidence`, `test_no_judgement_is_written_for_a_target_that_produced_no_outcome`, `test_a_judgement_about_a_case_with_no_target_outcome_is_refused`, `test_a_judgement_about_an_unreplayed_case_is_refused`, `test_a_full_replay_passes_the_gate_and_binds_exactly_what_it_claims`, `test_a_target_naming_a_rubric_that_cannot_grade_it_is_never_substituted`, `test_zz_control_the_untampered_record_still_reviews_and_applies_with_two_rubrics` |

| A token must not be shown for an apply that will be refused | The store's `read_text`, which decides whether `review` prints a token, whether `apply` accepts the base and what base the improver and the validation see, opens the document itself with `O_NOFOLLOW` and `O_NONBLOCK` and judges the open handle: a link is `LinkedTargetRefused` and a fifo or any other non-regular file is `BaseHashMismatch`, both before any token or text is shown, and a fifo opens instead of waiting for a writer; line endings are folded as the text-mode read before it did. | The text listing must say what the JSON says | `skills` prints every discovery warning (executable payloads beside a document, a frontmatter name that disagrees with its directory, `disable-model-invocation`, bytes that are not UTF-8) indented under the skill it belongs to, mirror copies labelled by root id, one line per warning; `_emit`, the choke point every text listing prints through, spells a line break inside a line as `\\n` rather than obeying it, so a skill directory or payload file named with a newline cannot author a listing line in `skills`, `candidates`, `discover`, `doctor` or the `daily` summary. Measured before: the same tree produced the payload warning in `skills --json` and nothing in `skills`, and with the lines first added an executable named `run\\n    warning: ...` printed as two warnings. A root holding several copies of one skill is one name with a count in the head line (`_root_labels`: root ids in first-seen order, each once, x2 past one), never the id once per copy, and each mirror copy under such a root is then listed by path on its own line with its own DIVERGENT tag; a root with one copy renders as before, with no copy lines; the ambiguous copies of an editable root stay on the why line by path and get no copy lines. The reviewer note `propose_candidate` writes for divergent mirrors names each by root id and path for the same reason. | A skill document's own frontmatter name is quoted masked and bounded | `_snapshot` (`adapters/skill_fs.py`) warns that a document's frontmatter name differs from its directory with the name through `quoted_value`, and `skill_text_defects` (`app/pipeline.py`) quotes the base document's name the same way when a replacement renames the skill, as it already quoted the replacement's: the `repr`, masked, then cut at `QUOTED_VALUE_CHARS` with the cut counted, so a name whose `repr` fits in 120 characters and holds no credential shape reads exactly as before. After: `skills` 824 bytes with a longest line of 223, the token masked in both, and the daily summary 1,179 bytes, the quarantine record 610, the checkpoint 1,392 and the receipt 9,195; a healthy tree's `skills`, `skills --json`, `daily`, `doctor` and records are the same as before. 8 mutants (either name quoted raw, unbounded, or cut without being masked; the base name cut without the count; the warning dropped) are each caught on both. Not covered by this row: `skills --json` keeps the snapshot's `frontmatter_name` and `frontmatter_description` whole, as the document's own values (`discover --json` keeps a thread whole the same way); the directory name in the warning stays as it was, as the head line above it prints it; and a base document holding a credential is refused before any model runs, so the masked base name is reached only through `skill_text_defects` itself | `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `app/pipeline.py` | `test_the_control_a_short_differing_name_is_warned_as_before`, `test_a_long_name_is_cut_on_its_line_and_the_cut_counted`, `test_a_token_in_the_name_is_masked_in_the_text_and_the_json_warning`, `test_the_json_snapshot_keeps_the_documents_own_name_whole`, `test_the_control_a_rename_is_refused_naming_both_names_as_before`, `test_a_long_base_name_is_cut_in_every_record_it_reaches`, `test_the_refusal_masks_a_token_in_the_base_name` |
| The model's prose must not be able to write the report that judges it | `review` prints the hypothesis, intended change, risks and rollback note as the model wrote them (masked, and clipped past the limits the row "An improver reply leaves a bounded candidate" states), line breaks kept, but indented: every line of that prose opens with a `  | ` margin (a blank line is a bare `  |`) or, for the first line of a risk, a `  - ` bullet (`quoted`); candidate id, skill id, path, gate reasons, evidence references, notes and mirror paths are kept to one line (`one_line`). The two `---`/`+++` labels of the diff are the other text a reviewer did not write, copied from the path by `difflib`, so `make_patch` spells a line break inside them (`one_line_label`), and because the integrity check `review_report` runs recomputes the diff through the same function, a stored diff whose label obeys a break is a mismatch, not a rendering. Only the report's own text stands at column 0: the `candidate`, `skill`, `file`, `base sha256` and `gate` field lines, the section headers, the prefixed lines of the diff and its `\ No newline at end of file` marker, `note:` lines and the sentence that introduces the apply command (the command itself is indented two spaces beneath it), so neither prose nor a directory name can imitate a `DIFF` header or the apply instruction, and the reviewer still sees everything the candidate keeps of what the model and the file system said. Measured before: a hypothesis containing `DIFF`, `(empty)` and `To promote this change ... --approve FORGEDTOKEN` printed a complete second DIFF-and-apply section above the real one; a skill directory named with the same text printed three | `cli.py`, `domain/candidate.py` | `test_the_forged_prose_reviews_and_is_shown_in_full`, `test_the_report_has_one_diff_one_apply_instruction_and_the_real_token`, `test_nothing_the_model_wrote_stands_at_column_zero`, `test_a_skill_directory_named_with_line_breaks_cannot_author_a_diff_header`, `test_every_line_of_the_diff_carries_a_diff_prefix`, `test_a_stored_diff_that_obeys_the_break_is_a_derived_field_mismatch` |
| Every changed line in the diff must be its own line | `make_patch` ends an unterminated diff line (one made from a last line that has no newline) with `\ No newline at end of file` on its own line, the marker `diff` and `git` print, so the removed and added forms of such a line never run together and the missing newline is itself visible; the line counts are unchanged and the integrity recomputation goes through the same function, so a stored diff in the run-together form is a mismatch (a record written before this rule against a file whose last line has no newline is therefore refused as `CandidateTampered` at review, and `asi daily --commit` proposes it afresh). Measured before: a base ending without a newline rendered `-last line+last line changed` as one removed line, and when the new text kept that line and added another, the added `+last line` disappeared into the removed line's text | `domain/candidate.py` | `test_a_changed_unterminated_last_line_is_two_lines_each_marked`, `test_an_added_line_after_an_unterminated_last_line_is_not_swallowed`, `test_a_stored_diff_in_the_joined_form_is_a_derived_field_mismatch`, `test_the_control_terminated_texts_carry_no_marker_and_are_unchanged` |
| A document discovery steps over must be reported, not silent | `FilesystemSkillStore` records every `SKILL.md` it saw and did not take as a `PassedOverDocument` (root id, path, reason: a link whose target does not exist or cannot be reached, a link while the store does not follow links, a link that resolves outside its root, a document deeper than `max_depth`, an entry that is not a regular file, named by kind: a directory, fifo, socket, device or otherwise; a directory the walk could not list, with the error) and exposes them as `passed_over()`, refreshed with the snapshots. Entries are enumerated with `os.walk`, not `rglob`, because on Python 3.11 a glob drops an entry whose `exists()` is false and a dangling link was never seen at all. `doctor` prints one `WARN` line per document (a warning, not a problem: a stepped-over document does not stop a run, and `doctor`'s exit code means only that the configuration is not runnable), counts them on its `skills` line and lists them as `skill_documents_passed_over` in JSON; `skills` prints one `passed over (<root>)` line each, counts them in its summary and lists them as `passed_over` in JSON. The fifo is judged by stat and never opened. Measured before: with a dangling link, an unfollowed link, a fifo and a too-deep document present, `doctor` said `verdict: ready` with no mention and `skills` listed exactly what a clean tree lists, so a skipped skill read as an absent one. A document the walk saw but this process cannot read (mode 000, or gone between the listing and the read) is passed over the same way, with the OS reason, and its mode is not touched; this closes the discovery read only (the propose stage reads the canonical copy again, so a document made unreadable mid-run is still a later cycle's concern). | An empty value never leaves a flag to swallow its neighbour, and the window is never empty | `render_argv` in `adapters/conversation_cli.py`, when it drops an argument that is one placeholder rendering empty, drops with it a literal flag (an argument beginning with a dash and carrying no placeholder, judged on the template, not the rendered value) that immediately precedes it; `--`, a bare dash and a negative number are kept. | A document is one text under one identity from proposal to rollback, whatever its line endings | Every hash of a skill document folds line endings (`sha256_text`), and `write_canonical` and `rollback` in `adapters/skill_fs.py` now hash the bytes on disk the same way (`_document_sha256`, which names bytes that are not UTF-8 as `UndecodableDocument` rather than as a changed base), so a CRLF document proposed from its folded text is accepted at apply. | A blocked run blocks the next one until somebody says they looked | `run_daily` reads the newest daily receipt (`StateStore.scan_receipts`) before the lock, before any record is written under the state tree and before a conversation is read (the command has preflighted the source, exported nothing; the layout and owner-only mode repair every run performs come first, so a receipt whose mode drifted is repaired and then read, never refused as unreadable); if its `mutation_detected` field is true (the field, not the status: a receipt without it does not block), the run is refused with `BlockedRunUnreviewed` unless `mutation_reviewed` names that run's day, and the command maps that refusal to `RC_BLOCKED` so the page repeats every morning instead of filing as an ordinary refusal. The run that names the day records the statement in its receipt notes and becomes the newest receipt, so the schedule resumes without a change to the cron line; a dry run proceeds on the statement but writes no receipt, so the block stands. The gate fails closed: a receipt that cannot be read (torn, or a mode this process cannot open, which `scan_receipts` lists rather than raises) refuses the run as `StateUnreadable` instead of falling through to the previous readable receipt, and a day or path read back from the tree is spelled onto one line (`one_line_label`) before it reaches the message. | A run that refuses still reports the modes it reset first | Wiring the runner opens the sandbox scratch root (a reset there is pending in the store until `ensure()` reports it) and `ensure()` repairs the whole tree before every refusal `run_daily` makes after its layout check (the blocked-run gate, an unreadable receipt, a non-file where the day's receipt or checkpoint goes), and a refused run writes no receipt. `cmd_daily` catches `AsiError` from the runner wiring onward (so a model command that is not found, an isolation refusal or a source that fails preflight report too), writes one stderr line through `_report_repairs_of_refused_run` naming the count and the reset paths (`StateStore.repairs_so_far`: what `ensure()` reported plus any reset still pending, the first five, each spelled onto one line by `one_line`) and that no receipt reports the repair, then re-raises, so the exit code and the refusal line are unchanged and follow it; a refusal that reset nothing adds no line, a run that proceeds reports the repair in its receipt as before, and an exception that is not a refusal (exit 70, a bug report) is left to its traceback. | Every command that opens the state tree names the modes it reset | `StateStore.dir` resets a drifted mode on the directory it opens and leaves the reset pending (`repairs_so_far` lists it) for `ensure()` to report; only `daily` calls `ensure()` and only `doctor` reports its own walk. Every store a command opens goes through `_open_state` in `cli.py`, which registers it, and `main` calls `_report_unreported_repairs` before it returns and before it writes any refusal, not-found or internal-error line: each registered store whose `repairs_reported` flag is unset gets one stderr line naming the count and the reset paths (through `one_line`) and that the repair changed no content, then the flag is set. `daily` sets the flag when its receipt reports the repair or when `_report_repairs_of_refused_run` has, and `doctor`'s own walk (`repair_permissions`) drains the pending list into its report, so neither says it twice; a clean tree adds no line, the note never touches stdout (a `--json` listing stays JSON), a directory a command did not open is neither reset nor named, and the registry is cleared when `main` returns, so an in-process second call never inherits the first call's store. Measured before: with the state directory and its candidates directory at 0755, `candidates`, `set-aside`, `review`, `discover`, `apply` and `rollback` each put the directory back to 0700 and none of them said so, on success or on refusal | `cli.py`, `app/state.py` | `test_a_listing_a_review_and_a_refused_promotion_each_name_the_reset`, `test_only_the_directories_a_command_opens_are_reset_and_named`, `test_a_clean_tree_adds_no_line_and_a_json_listing_stays_json`, `test_daily_and_doctor_report_once_in_their_own_output_not_twice`, `test_an_in_process_second_call_does_not_inherit_the_first_calls_store` |
| The run that says a mutation was reviewed must not destroy the record of it | The receipt path is per day, so a committed run of a day whose receipt recorded a mutation (the one started with `--mutation-reviewed` naming that day after a morning review, or a later re-run of that day once a newer run has cleared the block) writes its receipt where the blocked one sits. `run_daily` keeps that receipt first, inside the lock and before anything else is written, deciding from the file at the path it will write (`StateStore.committed_receipt_at`, which reads that path itself, so the newest receipt being another day's, or the name being spelled differently on a case-insensitive filesystem, changes nothing; a file there that does not read as a receipt is a `StateUnreadable` refusal by name): `StateStore.keep_blocked_receipt` moves it (`os.replace`, never a copy) to `StateStore.kept_blocked_receipt_path`, `_blocked--` then the keyed day and the finish stamp (a leading underscore, which `safe_name` never leaves on a keyed day, so a run day literally named blocked still has a receipt that counts), suffixed when anything already holds that name so no earlier kept receipt is replaced; the new receipt's note names the kept path and the command's report prints it. A kept receipt is an archive, never a run: `scan_receipts` passes every `_blocked--*.json` over by name (`is_kept_receipt_name`) and lists it under `kept`, so a kept receipt stamped ahead of the clock or sorting ahead on a tie can never re-block the schedule, and `doctor` warns with their count and paths (`kept_blocked_receipts` under `--json`) so they do not accumulate unseen. A path that holds no receipt recording a mutation is overwritten as it always was; a dry run moves nothing; a store that withholds writes refuses the move as a `SafetyRefusal`, and a move the tree refuses (an `OSError`) is the same named refusal with both paths, never a traceback. Measured before: the reviewed run overwrote the blocked receipt, and `skill_tree.changes`, the only record of what wrote to the tree, was gone, destroyed by the run that said it was reviewed | `app/daily.py`, `app/state.py`, `cli.py` | `test_the_blocked_receipt_is_kept_byte_for_byte_and_the_new_receipt_says_where`, `test_an_older_blocked_receipt_at_the_run_day_is_kept_whichever_run_is_newest`, `test_the_same_entry_under_another_spelling_is_kept_not_clobbered`, `test_a_kept_receipt_is_never_read_as_a_run_again`, `test_a_day_named_blocked_still_blocks_its_receipt_is_not_a_kept_one`, `test_a_run_that_dies_after_the_keep_leaves_exactly_one_copy`, `test_the_store_reads_what_sits_at_the_days_path_and_refuses_what_does_not_read`, `test_a_blocked_receipt_the_run_would_not_overwrite_is_left_where_it_is`, `test_an_ordinary_same_day_re_run_still_overwrites_and_keeps_nothing`, `test_a_dry_run_of_the_same_day_moves_nothing`, `test_a_kept_name_already_taken_is_not_replaced`, `test_the_store_refuses_to_move_a_receipt_while_withholding_writes`, `test_a_receipt_without_a_finish_time_is_kept_as_unfinished`, `test_a_move_the_tree_refuses_is_a_named_refusal_and_the_receipt_stays`, `test_the_reviewed_run_of_the_blocked_day_keeps_its_receipt_and_doctor_counts_it` |
| A promotion and a run never overlap | `apply_candidate` and `rollback_candidate` hold the run lock (`RunLock` at `StateStore.lock_path`, the one `run_daily` takes) from the live read of the skill (for rollback, from the choice of the backup manifest) through the skill write to the candidate record and the action receipt (`_holding_the_run_lock`), with an owner line naming the promotion, so a run started meanwhile is refused `LockHeld` naming it; a promotion that finds the lock held is refused `LockHeld` naming the action, the candidate and the holder, before anything is read from or written to the tree, the backups or the records, and the attempt leaves the lock free; `rollback` refuses inside an automated run (`AutomationRefused`) as `apply` always has, before the lock. The holder line is read back from the lock file, so it is spelled onto one line (`one_line_label`) before it reaches the message. Measured before: `apply` took no lock, an apply during a scheduled run went through, the run finished `blocked` with the applied document in `skill_tree.changes`, its candidates were closed as blocked-run, and the next morning's run was refused until the operator reviewed a mutation the tool had made itself | `app/promote.py`, `app/lock.py` | `test_apply_and_rollback_refuse_while_a_run_holds_the_lock_and_write_nothing`, `test_the_lock_names_the_promotion_and_a_run_meanwhile_is_refused_naming_it`, `test_a_lock_held_refusal_is_raised_with_the_action_and_the_lock_released`, `test_rollback_inside_an_automated_run_is_refused_as_automation_before_the_lock`, `test_the_backup_manifest_is_chosen_under_the_lock_not_before_it` |
| A wrapper that answers wrongly fails verification, it does not crash it | `verify` never raises for a failed verification: the probe reply is read through `_probe_reply`, which accepts only an object carrying exactly the string fields `PROBE_KEYS` names, the ones `PROBE_SOURCE` writes, so a wrapper (`bwrap` from PATH, `sandbox-exec` at its fixed path) that prints a non-object literal, JSON nested past the recursion limit, an object with non-string fields or without the three keys (which used to be read as the sandbox failing every check), or bytes that are not UTF-8 (the probe is read with `errors="replace"`) is one more shape of the probe not having run, and the mechanism is refused as unverified rather than aborting the run as an internal error | `adapters/sandbox.py` | `test_a_probe_reply_that_is_valid_json_but_not_an_object_did_not_run`, `test_a_probe_reply_nested_past_the_recursion_limit_did_not_run`, `test_a_probe_reply_whose_fields_are_not_strings_did_not_run`, `test_a_probe_reply_without_the_probe_keys_did_not_run`, `test_a_probe_reply_that_is_not_utf8_did_not_run`, `test_the_control_shows_the_reply_the_real_probe_writes_is_accepted`, `test_a_wrapper_that_prints_a_non_object_fails_verification_instead_of_raising` |
| A number that is not a finite double is an unreadable record, not an aborted run | `json.loads` accepts the non-standard tokens NaN, Infinity and -Infinity by default and parses a legal literal that does not round to a finite double (1e400) to infinity; one such position reached `int()` in the normalizer and aborted a committed run as an internal error with no receipt. `loads_untrusted` now reads every float literal through `_finite_float` and the three tokens through `_refuse_constant` (integer literals are untouched), so the text is a `ValueError` the caller already classifies: the fixtures source passes the export over by name with the number in the reason, the healthy export beside it is scored, a model reply carrying one is `MalformedModelOutput`, a state leaf carrying one is `StateUnreadable`; `_as_int` and `_opt_int` in the normalizer tolerate a non-finite float handed to them directly, and `dumps` refuses to write one (`allow_nan=False`) so a writer bug fails at write time rather than as a leaf no reader can read | `jsonio.py`, `adapters/transcript_export.py` | `test_the_non_standard_tokens_are_a_value_error`, `test_a_literal_past_the_double_range_is_a_value_error`, `test_the_control_shows_ordinary_numbers_still_read_as_before`, `test_a_refused_number_is_a_plain_value_error_like_deep_nesting`, `test_a_non_finite_position_sorts_as_the_default_rather_than_raising`, `test_a_non_finite_optional_int_is_absent_rather_than_raising`, `test_the_run_completes_and_scores_the_healthy_record`, `test_the_spoiled_export_is_named_in_the_receipt_with_its_number`, `test_a_model_reply_with_a_non_finite_confidence_is_malformed_not_a_crash`, `test_a_state_leaf_with_a_non_finite_number_is_a_named_refusal`, `test_the_writer_refuses_a_non_finite_float_rather_than_emitting_a_token_no_reader_takes`, `test_the_control_shows_the_writer_still_writes_ordinary_floats` |

| A scorer's evidence or confidence field of the wrong shape is a recorded defect, not an aborted run | `_build_score` reads a criterion's `evidence` through `_evidence_list`, which hands back the array the contract asks for, an empty list for null, and for any other shape (a number, a boolean, a string, an object) appends one malformed ref naming the type found and hands back nothing, so a decided verdict on a criterion that requires evidence is downgraded to INSUFFICIENT the way a malformed ref always sends it; `_confidence` reads the stored `confidence` as a float only when it is a number a float can hold, and as none otherwise; the persisted reason spells out at most `MAX_SHOWN_BAD_REFS` refs and counts the rest. After: each exits 3, every file unchanged, and a healthy apply and rollback prints, writes and records what it did before. 11 mutants (no binding passed; the document unchecked, or compared unresolved; the hashes unchecked, only the base or only the applied hash compared, or the two swapped; every mismatch let through; the record checked only after the backup is judged; the applied hash quoted raw; the document named raw) are each caught on both. | A malformed value typed on the command line is masked and bounded in the refusal that names it | A value typed on the command line reaches its refusal through `quoted_value` (its `repr`, masked by the redaction shapes, cut at `QUOTED_VALUE_CHARS` with the count left unquoted): the run day in `validate_run_day` (`--day`, `--mutation-reviewed`), and the hyphenated day its hint repeats, the candidate id in `StateStore.candidate_path` (what `review`, `apply` and `rollback` look up), the page size in `_positive_int_argument`, the value argparse calls an invalid choice (`_EscapingParser._check_value`, a subcommand, a `set-aside --kind` or a scheduler flavor, with the choices still listed) and the words it does not recognize (`main` parses known arguments and quotes at most `QUOTED_EXTRA_ARGUMENTS` of the rest, counting the others); argparse's own messages that repeat the typed word whole, an ambiguous option (`daily --co=<value>`) and an explicit argument to a flag that takes none (`daily --commit=<value>`), are masked and, past `ARGPARSE_MESSAGE_CHARS`, cut in the middle with a count of the characters left out, keeping the end where argparse names the matches (`_EscapingParser.error`, `_bounded_argparse_message`); a page size spelled as int() reads it but with more digits than Python reads from text is told the digit limit instead of that it is not a whole number; a short value with nothing to mask reads as before, except an unrecognized word, which argparse printed bare and which is now quoted like the others. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): a 100,000-character `--day`, `--mutation-reviewed`, candidate id, `--limit`, subcommand, `set-aside --kind`, scheduler flavor, unknown option, ambiguous option or explicit argument to a flag each came back whole on stderr, about 100 KB; a credential pasted where `asi review` takes a candidate id, typed as a `--day` or after `--commit=`, was printed as typed; and a `--limit` of 5,000 nines was refused as not a whole number.
