| The leak scan's hostname and jwt rules, and its line numbers, take time in proportion to the text | `scan_text` in the scanner finds each rule's matches through `_matches`: a rule with a `start` and a `run` (the `jwt` rule: `eyJ` at a word boundary, then token characters) is matched at each start and, when a start has no match, resumes where the run after it ends, as the redactor's jwt shape does; the `corp-hostname` rule starts only where a run of host characters starts (a lookbehind), since a match from inside a run also matches from its start; a finding's line is bisected from the line-break offsets, listed once per text; `selftest` checks that a rule with a start has a run and that its start finds its own positive sample. The matches found are the ones the patterns alone give. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): 32 KiB of letters and dashes took 3 to 4 s in the hostname rule (256 KiB was still running at three minutes) and 256 KiB of `-eyJ` starts 12 to 17 s in the jwt rule, each doubling four times as long; a tracked file may be up to 2 MB and the history patch is read whole, so by that growth a 2 MB file of such text would take hours in the hostname rule and minutes in the jwt rule (extrapolated, not run); and numbering 40,000 findings in 1.5 MiB took about 10 s, also four times as long per doubling. Not covered by this row: the scanner's other rules, which this change did not measure | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_a_long_run_of_host_characters_is_scanned_in_linear_time`, `test_text_full_of_jwt_starts_is_scanned_in_linear_time`, `test_many_findings_are_numbered_in_linear_time`, `test_the_control_each_rule_finds_what_its_old_pattern_found`, `test_the_control_a_finding_is_on_the_line_counted_from_the_start`, `test_selftest_refuses_a_start_without_a_run_and_a_start_that_skips_its_sample` |
| A rubric version below 1 is refused when the rubric loads | `Rubric.from_mapping` refuses a `[rubric].version` that is not a whole number of at least 1, the bound `RubricBinding` holds every stored candidate to, so `doctor` and `daily` name the rubric file and write nothing. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): with `version = 0` or `-5`, `doctor` said ready, `daily --commit` exited 0 with a validated candidate, `candidates` listed it with 0 unreadable records, and `review` exited 3 with `CandidateTampered`, saying the record had changed since it was written, which it had not | `domain/rubric.py` | `test_a_rubric_version_below_one_is_refused_at_load_by_its_file`, `test_doctor_and_daily_refuse_a_version_below_one_before_anything_is_written`, `test_the_control_a_version_of_one_or_more_is_reviewed_as_written` |
| A stop signal ends the model command with the run | `main` runs each command inside `_stop_signals_unwind`, which turns SIGTERM and SIGHUP (`STOP_SIGNALS`), when still at their default action, into `Stopped`, a `BaseException` raised in the main thread, so the `finally` in `run_bounded` ends the model or source command's process group, the lock is released and a half-written file removed; the command exits 128 plus the signal number with `stopped by SIGTERM` on stderr. The handlers stay taken over while a failure or the stop is reported, a second stop signal during the unwind is let pass, and the process's own command (`entry`, behind `asi` and `python -m agent_skill_improver`) leaves the stop signals ignored after a stop until it exits, so a burst of them still exits as the stop. Ctrl-C stays Python's own `KeyboardInterrupt`, raised at each press, and a stop signal during its unwind is let pass. A stop that arrives after `run_bounded` starts the command and before its read loop begins still ends the command, and a temporary file is named before it is created (`write_bytes_atomic`, `_write_pinned`), so a stop that lands as it is created still has it removed; it is created owner-only (0600), as `tempfile.mkstemp` created it, and a name another writer holds is left alone. A signal the caller ignores stays ignored (`nohup`), `main` puts back the handlers it found, off the main thread nothing is installed, and a command that catches every `Exception` cannot catch it either. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): SIGTERM or SIGHUP during a model call ended the run with no message (exit -15, -1), and the model command went on running, re-parented to process 1 and past its own timeout (alive 6 s after the signal with a 3 s timeout), until it wrote its answer to an output nobody read and ended on the broken pipe; Ctrl-C already ended it with the run. Measured with the first form of this change (3.11 and 3.13), which put the handlers back as the command ended: 40 SIGTERMs 2 ms apart printed the stop, then ended the run by the signal, exit -15, three times of three; and one of twelve SIGTERMs at random moments of an `apply` (3.13) left a `.tmp-*.part` file in the state's candidates directory, created by `tempfile.mkstemp` before the write's cleanup could name it. Not covered by this row: a signal that arrives while Python is still starting, before `main` runs, or after the command has returned, which ends the process by the signal as before (exit -15); a process the model command starts in a session of its own (`setsid`), which no group kill reaches; and an `apply` stopped after it wrote the skill and before it marked the candidate applied (measured once in those twelve), which the row on a stopped `apply` covers | `cli.py`, `adapters/bounded_process.py`, `jsonio.py`, `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_sigterm_or_sighup_mid_model_call_ends_the_model_command_with_the_run`, `test_after_a_stop_the_next_daily_runs_and_no_partial_file_is_left`, `test_the_control_a_hangup_nohup_ignores_still_does_not_stop_the_run`, `test_the_control_ctrl_c_still_ends_the_model_command_with_the_run`, `test_a_second_signal_while_the_first_unwinds_does_not_cut_the_cleanup_short`, `test_a_command_that_catches_every_error_still_does_not_catch_a_stop`, `test_main_restores_the_handlers_it_found_and_runs_off_the_main_thread`, `test_a_burst_of_stop_signals_still_exits_as_the_stop`, `test_the_process_entry_leaves_the_stops_ignored_and_main_puts_them_back`, `test_a_stop_signal_while_ctrl_c_unwinds_is_let_pass`, `test_the_control_each_ctrl_c_still_raises_as_pythons_own_does`, `test_a_stop_before_the_read_loop_still_ends_the_child`, `test_a_stop_while_a_failure_is_reported_is_a_stop_too`, `test_a_stop_as_a_temporary_file_is_created_leaves_no_temporary_file`, `test_the_control_a_temporary_name_already_taken_is_left_alone`, `test_a_temporary_file_is_owner_only_from_the_moment_it_is_created` |
| A skill document whose path is longer than the system opens is passed over, by its length | `_iter_skill_files` in `adapters/skill_fs.py` takes an entry whose `lstat` is refused with `File name too long` as a document passed over, with its length in bytes as the reason, and as a copy of the skill it would be, so that skill's canonical slot is withheld and a proposal against the skill is quarantined, naming the copy and its length; `doctor` names it in a warning and stays ready, and `daily` runs to its receipt. Any other error from that call is raised as before. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): a canonical root reached through a link to a deep directory, so that an entry resolved to 1,024 bytes, made `doctor` and `daily` fall with `OSError: [Errno 63] File name too long`, exit 70; at 1,023 bytes both ran | `adapters/skill_fs.py` | `test_an_entry_past_the_path_limit_is_passed_over_by_its_length`, `test_daily_runs_and_names_the_canonical_copy_it_passed_over`, `test_the_control_an_entry_at_the_path_limit_is_inventoried`, `test_any_other_refusal_of_that_call_is_raised_as_before` |
| An `apply` stopped after it wrote the skill is finished by running it again | `write_canonical` writes the backup manifest before the skill rather than after it, and removes it where the skill is known to hold its original text again (a write the platform refused, a write that did not verify and was put back). An `apply` that finds the skill no longer at the candidate's base records the apply instead of refusing when the skill holds exactly the candidate's new text and the newest backup manifest of this candidate records that write, from the candidate's base to that text, for the same document (`_earlier_write_of`, `earlier_write`); the record's own integrity is checked as on a first run (`verify_full_integrity`) but not against the base text, which the skill no longer holds and which the earlier run checked before it wrote; the approval token is verified before anything is recorded (a wrong one is `ApprovalRequired`); and the report says `finishes` and the receipt `finishes_an_earlier_run`. Where those conditions do not hold, the apply is refused with `BaseHashMismatch` as before: a manifest that records another base, another text or another document or cannot be read, and an edit made after the stop, even with the manifest edited to record it. The apply's receipt is written before the candidate is marked applied, so a stop between the two leaves a candidate the next apply finishes. A `rollback` of a `validated` candidate whose skill still holds its base is refused as never applied (`_still_at_its_base`), since the manifest written ahead of the skill outlives an apply stopped before its skill write. A stop signal during `apply` or `rollback` adds a line saying to run the same command again (`FINISH_BY_RUNNING_AGAIN`; tested with SIGTERM, and SIGHUP takes the same path), which for `apply` also says that if the stopped run had recorded the apply, the rerun is refused as already `applied` with nothing left to do; a `rollback` stopped after its restore is finished by running it again, as before, by finding the previous text in place. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): an `apply` stopped by SIGTERM as its skill write returned left the skill holding the candidate's text, the candidate `validated`, the copy taken before the write and no manifest, so `rollback` answered that the candidate was never applied (`GateFailed`) and the next `apply` was refused with `BaseHashMismatch`, saying the skill had changed since the proposal, each exit 3; an apply left after its manifest and before its record (made by applying and putting the record back) was refused the same way, while `doctor` said ready and the next `daily` ran `ok`. Measured with the first form of this change (3.13): a `rollback` after an apply stopped before its skill write reported `already_restored`, closed the candidate as `rolled-back` and left its apply refused. Not covered by this row: Ctrl-C, which raises Python's own `KeyboardInterrupt` with no such line, though running the same apply again finishes it the same way (measured on 3.13); `doctor`, which says ready over an apply left this way and does not name it; `review`, which still refuses such a candidate with `BaseHashMismatch`; and a crash (SIGKILL, power loss), which leaves the same files at each of these points but was not run | `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `app/promote.py`, `cli.py` | `test_an_apply_stopped_as_its_skill_write_returned_is_finished_by_running_it_again`, `test_an_apply_stopped_as_it_records_its_write_is_finished_too`, `test_the_second_run_still_needs_the_candidates_token`, `test_the_control_an_edit_after_the_stopped_apply_is_refused_as_before`, `test_an_edit_the_manifest_is_edited_to_record_is_refused_too`, `test_a_manifest_that_does_not_record_this_write_is_not_taken_for_it`, `test_the_control_a_write_the_platform_refuses_leaves_no_manifest`, `test_the_control_a_write_that_does_not_verify_and_is_put_back_leaves_no_manifest`, `test_a_rollback_after_an_apply_stopped_before_its_skill_write_leaves_it_to_apply`, `test_rollback_restores_an_apply_stopped_as_its_skill_write_returned`, `test_a_stopped_rollback_says_to_run_it_again_and_running_it_again_finishes_it`, `test_a_stop_after_the_apply_was_recorded_says_the_rerun_has_nothing_to_do`, `test_the_control_a_stop_of_another_command_says_nothing_of_running_it_again` |
| A forged delimiter is neutralized in time in proportion to the text | `wrap_untrusted` finds a forged delimiter with `_FENCE_RE`, which tries a run of `<` from its first one only (a lookbehind: what follows the run is never a `<`, so a start inside the run fails whenever its first one did) and has one way to split the space around the optional slash, where the old pattern had one for each place in it. Each match the old pattern found begins, at the same place, with a match of this one, and each of this one's lies inside one of the old one's, compared on 20,000 random strings of delimiter pieces and Unicode space (this one's end at the fence word and its `>`, as the row "A forged delimiter hides no text but its own" says). Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): a run of `<` took 0.29 to 0.33 s to fence at 4,000 characters, 1.2 to 1.3 s at 8,000, 4.7 to 5.2 s at 16,000 and 10.4 to 11.6 s at 24,000 (over two runs each), the default `max_skill_text_chars` a skill text is clipped to before it is fenced into a prompt, on which config sets no upper limit; `<<` and then 24,000 spaces took 4.7 to 6.2 s. After, on both, 24,000 of either took about a millisecond. Not covered by this row: the conversation source's workspace line and the leak scan's sampled-title rule, which this change did not alter (measured: each takes time in the square of a run of spaces) | `app/prompts.py` | `test_a_long_run_of_angle_brackets_is_fenced_in_linear_time`, `test_a_long_run_of_space_after_two_angle_brackets_is_fenced_in_linear_time`, `test_the_control_a_whole_run_of_angle_brackets_is_neutralized_with_the_word`, `test_the_control_each_match_of_the_old_pattern_begins_with_one_of_this_one` |
| A listing's workspace line is read in time in proportion to it | `parse_discovery` reads a thread's workspace with `_WORKSPACE_LINE`, whose path ends at its last character that is not space, so the space after the path is tried once rather than again from each place in it; a line with nothing but space after the word still reads as its last space character. The paths read are the ones the old pattern read, compared on 30,000 random lines of workspace pieces and Unicode space. Measured before, through `parse_discovery` (3.11 and 3.13, over two runs each): a workspace with a run of 4,000 spaces inside took 0.03 to 0.05 s, 8,000 took 0.12 to 0.21 s, 16,000 took 0.51 to 0.83 s and 32,000 took 2.0 to 3.3 s, four times as long per doubling; the listing is the conversation source's output, read up to 32 MiB (`MAX_EXPORT_BYTES`, discovery included). After, on both, 32,000 took under a millisecond. Not covered by this row: the leak scan's sampled-title rule, which this change did not alter | `adapters/conversation_cli.py` | `test_a_workspace_with_a_long_run_of_space_inside_is_read_in_linear_time`, `test_the_control_a_workspace_reads_as_it_did`, `test_the_control_the_pattern_reads_what_its_old_pattern_read` |
| The leak scan's sampled-title rule takes time in proportion to the text | The `sampled-thread-title` rule in the scanner has one way to split the space around its optional dash or underscore, where the old pattern had one for each place in it; the matches are the ones the old pattern found, compared on 8,000 random texts of title pieces. The scanner's other 26 rules were read for the same shapes, a run of space that can be split more than one way and a start inside a run that fails whenever the run's first one did, and none was found; timed through `scan_text` on 65 inputs built for those shapes, at 8,000 to 64,000 characters (3.11 and 3.13), each took about twice as long per doubling (two that grew faster in a single timing under a millisecond grew about twice per doubling when timed again, best of five, out to 512,000), and the slowest took under 6 ms at 64,000. Measured before, through `scan_text` (3.11 and 3.13, two runs each): the title's first word and then a run of 4,000 spaces took 0.033 to 0.047 s, 8,000 took 0.128 to 0.176 s, 16,000 took 0.508 to 0.690 s and 32,000 took 2.01 to 2.79 s, about four times as long per doubling; a tracked file may be up to 2 MB and the history patch is read whole, so by that growth a run of 2,000,000 spaces, a 2 MB file of them, would take 2.2 to 3.0 hours (extrapolated, not run). After, on both, 32,000 took about a millisecond | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_a_long_run_of_space_after_the_sampled_titles_first_word_is_scanned_in_linear_time`, `test_the_control_the_sampled_title_rule_finds_what_its_old_pattern_found` |
| A config or rubric nested too deeply for the TOML parser is a config error, not a fall | `load_config` parses the config file and each enabled rubric with `tomllib`, which calls itself for each array or inline table nested in a value and raises `RecursionError` a few hundred deep; that is not a `ValueError`, so neither parse turned it into a config error. Each parse now catches it too and refuses the file as invalid TOML, naming the file and the reason (`TOML_TOO_DEEP`); the file is read before the parse, so a `RecursionError` from anything but the parse is not reported as nesting. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13): `tomllib` alone fell at 497 nested arrays and at 331 nested inline tables, and `doctor` and `daily` first fell at 493 or 494 nested arrays and at 328 or 329 inline tables, in a config value or in a rubric, exit 70 with a traceback from inside the parser. At 400 nested arrays (3.13) the same config was refused for its unknown table, exit 3. After, on both, nestings at 11 depths of arrays and 10 of inline tables, from 2 to 1,000, placed in an unknown table, in three settings and in a rubric, were refused with exit 3 and no traceback by `doctor` and by `daily`, 210 runs on each Python. Not covered by this row: JSON, which `loads_untrusted` already refuses by name when nested too deeply | `config.py` | `test_a_config_value_nested_too_deeply_is_a_config_error`, `test_a_rubric_value_nested_too_deeply_is_a_config_error`, `test_the_control_a_shallow_nesting_is_read_and_refused_for_what_it_says`, `test_a_recursion_error_from_reading_the_file_is_not_called_nesting` |
| An unknown config key is quoted, masked and cut in its refusal | `_refuse_unknown_keys` in the config loader, and the refusal `[source]` words for itself, name each key they refuse through `_named`: bare when it is plain, short and nothing in it is masked, so `unknown key(s): extra` and `max_open_candidate (did you mean max_open_candidates?)` read as before, else quoted, masked and cut (`quoted_value`), as the rubric loader already quoted the keys it refuses. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13), through `doctor` in `[caps]` and through `doctor` and `daily` in `[source]`: a quoted key holding a line break and then `config error: forged` printed as two refusal lines, a `ghp_` token used as a key was printed whole, and a 1,000,000-character key made `doctor`'s refusal 1,000,391 bytes in `[caps]` and 1,000,497 in `[source]`; a terminal escape in a key was already spelled when printed. After, on both: one line with the key quoted, the token as `[REDACTED:github-token]`, and `doctor`'s refusal 545 and 651 bytes for the long key. Not covered by this row: how many unknown keys a refusal lists (100,000 short ones in `[caps]` made a refusal of 900,389 bytes before and after, and took about half a second longer after, one run each) | `config.py` | `test_a_line_break_in_an_unknown_key_cannot_start_a_line`, `test_a_credential_shaped_unknown_key_is_masked`, `test_a_long_unknown_key_is_cut`, `test_each_of_several_unknown_keys_is_named_on_its_own`, `test_the_control_a_plain_unknown_key_is_still_named_bare` |
| A skill root id is refused at load unless it can be printed and stored as written | `_load_skill_roots` checks each `[[skill_root]].id` with `_refuse_unusable_root_id` before any other use of it: 1 to 64 letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`, starting with a letter or digit (`_ROOT_ID`, the alphabet and length of `RUN_DAY_RE`), and nothing redaction would mask; the refusal names the id quoted, masked and cut (`quoted_value`). This refuses configs that loaded before: an id with a space, a colon, a leading `.`, `_` or `-`, a letter outside ASCII, more than 64 characters, or a shape redaction masks (such as `sk-` followed by 20 or more characters). Measured before (3.11 and 3.13), through `doctor`: an id holding a line break and then `config error: forged` made the `precedence` and `canonical` refusals print as two lines; a `ghp_` token used as an id was printed whole by six refusals (those two, a duplicate id, a missing path, an unknown kind and one root inside another), and, in a config that otherwise loads, by `skills`, and was written into the receipt and the candidate of a committed `daily`; a 1,000,000-character id made each of the six refusals about 1,000,000 bytes (1,000,041 to 1,000,446 here; the refusal for one root inside another holds two absolute root paths, so its size varies with where the roots are); `can:onical` loaded and labelled the payload files beside its skills `can:onical:<file>` in `skills --json`. After, on both: each is refused at load on one line, 308 bytes for the line break and the token and 437 for the long id, and the committed `daily` writes no receipt. Not covered by this row: a skill root's path, which the refusal for one root inside another prints as written (measured: a path holding a line break made it three lines); the row after this one covers it | `config.py` | `test_a_root_id_with_a_line_break_is_refused_on_one_line`, `test_a_credential_shaped_root_id_is_refused_masked_and_never_stored`, `test_a_root_id_longer_than_sixty_four_is_refused_and_cut`, `test_a_root_id_with_a_colon_a_space_or_a_leading_mark_is_refused`, `test_the_control_an_id_of_that_shape_still_loads_and_runs` |
| A config refusal that names a path with a line break in it stays on one line | `ConfigError` spells a line break in its message as `\\n` or `\\r` when it is built (`errors.py`), so every refusal of that kind is one line wherever it is printed or stored: the ones that name a skill root's path, the config file's own path or a rubric file's path, and any written later. No `ConfigError` is raised with a line break of its own (a scan of every `raise` in the package found line breaks only in refusals of other kinds), so nothing is lost; the refusals of other kinds that list their reasons one per line (`SafetyRefusal` and the like) keep that layout. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13), through `doctor` and `skills`, each path holding a line break and then `config error: forged`: a `mirror` root nested inside `canonical` printed its refusal as 3 lines, 2 of them starting `config error: forged`; a rubric directory, a missing config file and a config file that is not UTF-8 each printed 2 lines, 1 of them forged. After, on both: 1 line each, none starting with the forged text. Not covered by this row: a config-supplied path that a refusal of another kind names is still printed as written. Measured (3.11 and 3.13), each path holding a line break and then `config error: forged`: a state directory that cannot be created made the `SafetyRefusal` from `app/state.py` 2 lines (1 forged) in `doctor` and `daily`; a state directory with one of its mandatory subdirectories replaced by a file made `daily`'s `SafetyRefusal` 3 lines (2 forged); a missing fixtures directory made `daily`'s `SourceUnavailable` from `adapters/fixture_source.py` 2 lines (1 forged); the row after this one covers these three | `errors.py` | `test_nested_skill_roots_whose_paths_hold_line_breaks_are_refused_on_one_line`, `test_a_rubric_directory_whose_path_holds_a_line_break_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_a_config_file_path_with_a_line_break_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_a_config_file_that_is_not_utf8_at_such_a_path_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_only_a_config_refusal_is_flattened_and_its_text_survives_a_rebuild` |
| A refusal naming a state or fixtures path with a line break in it stays on one line | `_shown` in `app/state.py` spells a line break (`\\n`, `\\r`) in every path a state-store refusal or layout reason names: the state directory, an ancestor in its way, a record, a receipt, a child directory, a checkpoint of the wrong shape, and each reason a state scan returns for another command to raise (a lock entry, an artifact path, an unreadable receipt). `app/lock.py` does the same for the run lock path in each of its refusals. `adapters/fixture_source.py` does the same for the fixtures directory and the entries it names. The refusals that list their reasons one per line keep those lines. A path holding a line break stays a supported config (a launchd template carries it exactly, `test_the_control_a_tab_and_a_plist_line_break_are_carried_exactly`), so it is spelled where it is named rather than refused at load. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13), each path holding a line break and then `config error: forged`: a `state_dir` that cannot be created made a 2-line refusal (1 forged) in `doctor` and `daily`, and 3 lines (2 forged) when the ancestor in its way held the break too; a `state_dir` with a mandatory subdirectory replaced by a file after `doctor` built the tree made `daily`'s refusal 3 lines (2 forged); a fresh tree whose first subdirectory is a file made `daily` print 5 lines, 1 forged; a missing `fixtures_dir` made `daily`'s refusal 2 lines (1 forged); `review` of an absent candidate printed 2 lines (1 forged); a checkpoint of the wrong shape and a directory at the run lock path each made `daily` print 3 lines (1 forged); the promote refusal of a receipt path that is a directory, called directly, was 2 lines (1 forged). After, on both: each is 1 line, the fresh-tree case 4 lines with none forged (a repair note and the layout refusal's own 3; 3 lines since the row "`daily` creates nothing beside a state layout it refuses" removed the repair that note reported), the checkpoint and lock cases 2 lines with none forged (a repair note and the refusal), and `doctor`'s reports of a missing `fixtures_dir` and of a directory at the lock path (exit 1, on stdout) had no forged line before or after. Each of the 45 spellings in the three modules (32 in `app/state.py`, 8 in `app/lock.py`, 5 in `adapters/fixture_source.py`) is pinned: putting any one back to the raw path fails a test (47 mutants with the two `_shown` helpers themselves, all caught on both). Not covered by this row: a refusal in another module that names a path under a skill root, the state directory or the fixtures directory (a name match still finds path-like fields not spelled where they are formed in `adapters/skill_fs.py`, the most, each path its refusals interpolate now named by the row "A refusal from the skill store names each path on its line, masked and bounded" (an OS error's text in them aside), `cli.py`, `config.py` (whose refusals the row before this one flattens), `app/pipeline.py`, `app/promote.py`, `app/daily.py`, `domain/validation.py` and a few single sites elsewhere, none reviewed site by site except `daily`'s queue-guard stand-down, which names the candidates directory and was measured to forge no line on either: its summary line is flattened when printed and its JSON escapes the break); the other characters Python splits lines on (`\\x0b`, `\\x0c`, `\\u2028` and so on), which pass through as before; and a path holding a backslash followed by `n`, which is printed the same as one holding a line break | `app/state.py`, `app/lock.py`, `adapters/fixture_source.py` | `test_a_state_dir_that_cannot_be_created_is_refused_on_one_line`, `test_a_state_dir_with_a_subdirectory_replaced_by_a_file_is_refused_on_one_line`, `test_the_layout_refusal_keeps_its_own_lines_and_spells_the_path_inside_them`, `test_a_missing_fixtures_dir_is_refused_on_one_line`, `test_an_unreadable_state_record_under_such_a_path_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_each_other_state_refusal_under_such_a_root_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_an_ancestor_reason_under_such_a_directory_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_a_fixtures_dir_that_cannot_be_listed_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_a_fixture_lookup_under_such_a_directory_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_reviewing_an_absent_candidate_under_such_a_state_dir_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_a_checkpoint_of_the_wrong_shape_under_such_a_state_dir_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_each_reason_a_state_scan_returns_names_its_path_on_one_line`, `test_each_run_lock_refusal_under_such_a_state_dir_is_named_on_one_line`, `test_a_line_break_and_a_carriage_return_are_both_spelled`, `test_the_control_a_path_without_a_line_break_is_named_exactly_as_written` |
| `doctor` is not ready when today's run could not name a candidate | `candidate_naming_problem` in `app/daily.py` asks the question `_assert_every_skill_can_be_named` asked (the row on a run day that could not name a candidate describes it): it forms each known skill's longest candidate id for a day and returns why the first one would not fit, or nothing. `daily` raises that reason with "No conversation was read or scored." appended, its output unchanged (exit code, stdout and stderr identical before and after on 3.11 and 3.13, dry and `--commit`, for both cases below). `doctor` asks it for today's UTC day, the day a run without `--day` uses, and reports the reason as a problem (exit 1, `verdict: problems found`, and in `--json` under `problems`); the run's refusal is that reason plus the one sentence. `doctor` now works out today once, so this check and its checks of today's checkpoint and receipt ask about the same day. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13), with `daily --day 2026-03-01` and `doctor` on today, both 10 characters: with a 186-character skill directory name (a copy of a skill that loads, or of one that does not), or a `[caps].max_candidates_per_run` of 170 digits (the edge for these skills), `doctor` exited 0 and said ready while `daily` exited 3; at 185 characters and 169 digits both exited 0. After, on both: each of the 84 runs above gives the one reason naming the directory at fault, on stderr in one line or as `doctor`'s one `PROBLEM`, with no traceback, that directory still at the mode it was given and every entry below it as found, modes included. `discover`, which opens the state directory too, measured the same way (the state directory or its parent at 0600 or 0000, fresh or built, 3.11 and 3.13): exit 70 with a traceback before in all 8 cases, the one reason in one line, exit 3, after; `skills`, `rubrics`, `scheduler-template` and `selftest` do not need it and exit 0 before and after, both Pythons. | A credential glued to an escape, an underscore or a non-ASCII letter is masked | Every shape in `domain/redaction.py` that began at `\b` begins at `_LEAD` instead: not right after an ASCII letter or digit, unless that letter or digit ends an escape (`\n`, `\t`, `\r`, `\b`, `\f` or `\v`; `\x` and two hex digits, `\u` and four, `\U` and eight; `%` and two; or an `m` after a digit, `;` or `[`, as a terminal colour code ends, whether or not the escape character is there). The six vendor-prefix shapes end at `_TAIL`, not right before an ASCII letter or digit, where `\b` had left a token followed by a non-ASCII letter unmasked (any of the six) and one followed by `_` unmasked (the AWS shape and the `gh`-prefixed GitHub one), the Slack shape masking only part of either. So a key after a line break in a `repr` is masked, and with it the refusals `quoted_value` writes (a model's verdict, schema or `conversation_id`, a typed run day or candidate id); so is a key after an escape in JSON text, after `%20` in a URL or after a colour code, a token with `_` or a non-ASCII letter on either side, and an assignment whose key name ends a longer name (`DB_` before the password key). That last reaches past environment files: any snake_case name ending in a key name the assignment shape knows now counts as that key name, so `csrf_token = request.form.get('csrf')` loses its value as `token = request.form.get('csrf')` already did; across this repository's own Python, Markdown and TOML files at HEAD (51,230 lines) the assignment shape masks 39 lines against 33 before, on both Pythons, and it replaces a value rather than driving a refusal. An `&` written `\u0026` or `%26` before a URL parameter has its value masked when either shape knows its name: by the URL parameter shape when that one does (see the row on a credential in a signed, escaped or nested URL), otherwise by the assignment shape when the `=` is written as such (`passwd`, `session_token`), which in JSON text takes the closing quote and what follows with it; a name neither knows (`private_key`), or a name only the assignment shape knows with its `=` written `%3D`, is left as it was. Glued to an ASCII letter or digit in any other way, a shape is still taken for part of a longer word and left alone. Each lead sits behind a lookahead on the characters the shape can begin with, for speed alone, and each of those characters has a glued sample (both cases where the shape ignores case). Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both): of 72 glued samples (7 shapes, each after a `repr` `\n` or `\t`, a JSON `\n` or `\u00e9`, a `%20`, a colour code raw or in JSON, `MY_TOKEN_` or a non-ASCII letter, or before `_prod`; and 2 password assignments, after `DB_` and after a JSON `\n`), 67 were left as they were; `review` given a candidate id holding a line break and a key printed the key; and a key after a line break was quoted whole by `quoted_value` for each of the 7 shapes. Measured after: 0 of 72, the 8 unglued controls masked before and after. 4 MiB of prose is masked in 0.47 s on 3.13 and 0.38 s on 3.11, against 0.41 s and 0.36 s before (2.7 s and 2.5 s without the lookahead). Not covered by this row: the leak scanner's own rules, which still begin at `\b`; a terminal sequence other than a colour code (`ESC[K`) and a key whose own first letter is written as an escape, which are still left as they were; and speed, which is measured here and not tested | `domain/redaction.py` | `test_a_token_glued_to_an_escape_an_underscore_or_a_letter_is_masked`, `test_a_token_followed_by_an_underscore_or_a_non_ascii_letter_is_masked`, `test_an_assignment_whose_name_ends_a_longer_name_or_follows_an_escape_is_masked`, `test_json_text_holding_a_key_after_a_line_break_is_masked`, `test_text_full_of_underscore_and_escape_jwt_starts_is_masked_in_linear_time`, `test_a_snake_case_name_ending_in_a_key_name_is_masked_as_the_key_name_is`, `test_a_url_parameter_after_an_escaped_ampersand_is_masked`, `test_a_model_value_with_a_line_break_before_a_key_is_quoted_masked`, `test_an_unknown_verdict_holding_a_key_after_a_line_break_is_refused_masked`, `test_a_candidate_id_typed_with_a_line_break_before_a_key_is_masked`, `test_the_control_a_shape_glued_to_an_ascii_letter_or_digit_is_left_alone`, `test_the_control_prose_and_longer_names_are_left_alone` |
| A credential in a signed, escaped or nested URL is masked, and only its value | The URL parameter shape in `domain/redaction.py` begins at `_URL_PARAM`: `?` or `&` as written, `&` as JSON text escapes it (`\u0026`), or either percent-encoded, as in a URL carried inside another one's parameter (`%3F`, `%26`); then an optional `X-Amz-` or `X-Goog-` prefix, a name it knows (`security-token` now among them), and `=` or `%3D`. Its value (`_URL_VALUE`, 8 characters or more as before) ends at the next parameter (`&`, `\u0026`, or `%26` before a name and `=` or `%3D`), at a `#`, or at a character a URL cannot hold as written: whitespace, a double quote, or a single quote followed by whitespace, the end of the text or another such character (`"`, `<`, `>`, `[`, `]`, `^`, a backtick, `{`, a vertical bar, `}`). It ends too at a backslash escape, escaped once or more, standing for an ASCII character Python counts as whitespace, a double quote, `#` or `&` (`\"`, `\n`, `\u000A`, `\u0022`, `\u0026`, either case of hex). Anything else is part of the value, escapes included (a backslash before a slash, `\u002B`, `\'`): the assignment shape cannot mask past a placeholder, so a value cut short at a character it can hold would leave the rest of the secret in the text. A run of backslashes is taken whole, so a long one is read once. So a signed S3 or Cloud Storage URL has its `X-Amz-Signature` or `X-Goog-Signature` masked and keeps every other parameter; a parameter after a JSON-escaped or percent-encoded `&` is masked whichever of the shape's names it has; a parameter of a nested URL is masked and its neighbours kept; a URL in double quotes, or in single quotes before a space, a bracket or the end of the text, keeps its closing quote and what follows it; and a session token written with a backslash before every slash, as PHP's `json_encode` writes JSON, is masked whole. The shape still replaces a value and never drives a refusal. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both): of 11 URLs holding a secret (a signed S3 URL's signature and its session token, a Cloud Storage signature, each signature again in JSON text, `sig` and `key` after `\u0026`, `sig` after `%26`, a `token` and a `sig` in a nested URL, and a plain `access_token`), 9 kept it, the two masked being the plain parameter and the session token (that one by the assignment shape); of 5 URLs whose text after the value must survive, 3 lost it to the placeholder (a URL in JSON with a key after it, one in single quotes, one whose next parameter follows `\u0026`) and 1 kept its secret (a nested URL whose next parameter follows `%26`). Measured after: 0 of 11 kept their secret; of the 5, 1 lost text, the single-quoted URL followed by `)`, whose `')` a URL can hold and so goes with the value, byte for byte as before. Against HEAD, with the same results on both Pythons, a secret split in two by each of 71 characters and escapes, under each of 11 names, in each of 8 settings (6,248 texts: a parameter in the middle, one first and last, after `\u0026` in JSON text, in JSON text that writes a backslash before every slash, nested, after `%26`, in single quotes before `)`, and before a space): the first half was left in 2,201 before and is left in none; some part is left in 1,144 against 2,332, and each of the 644 second halves left that HEAD masked follows a stop listed above (a double quote, a single quote before one, a `#` in JSON text, `%26` before a name and `=` or `%3D`, `\"`, `\n`, `\t`, `\u0020`, `\u0022`, `\u0023`, `\u0026`), where by the URL's own reading the value has ended. 4 MiB of prose, and of URL-heavy text, is masked in about a tenth more time than before (best of 5: 0.48 s and 0.55 s on 3.13, 0.39 s and 0.47 s on 3.11, against 0.44 s and 0.50 s, and 0.37 s and 0.43 s, before), and text built to strain the value (one run of 4 Mi backslashes, 4 Mi single quotes, `%26` before long names that have no `=`, unfinished `\u` escapes) in time linear in its length (3.13: each doubling about twice as long). 32 mutants (each start spelling dropped, `\u0026`, `%3F` or `%26`; `%3D` dropped; no vendor prefix, or `X-Amz-` alone; no `security-token`; a value of any length; the old shape back, and the first cut of this value back; the value running past every `%26`, stopping at any `%26`, or stopping at one before a name with no `=` or only before a raw `=`; running past a double quote; a single quote always going with the value or always ending it, or going on before whitespace, the end of the text, a bracket, a double quote, or `<` and `>`; any backslash ending it, every run going on, a run not taken whole, the escape letters read in either case, and each of `\u0026`, `\u0022`, `\u000A`, upper-case hex, `\n` and `\"` going on) are each caught on both by `tests/test_redaction.py` and the modules of this row and of the row on a credential glued to an escape. Not covered by this row: a parameter name the shape does not know (`X-Goog-Credential` among them); an `=` or `&` written any other way (`\u003d`, or percent-encoded twice); a single quote before a character a URL can hold, such as `)` or `,`, which goes with the value; and a secret that itself holds one of the stops above (a raw double quote, `%26` before a name and `=`) | `domain/redaction.py` | `test_a_signed_cloud_store_url_has_its_signature_and_session_token_masked`, `test_the_parameters_beside_the_signature_are_kept`, `test_a_parameter_after_an_ampersand_written_as_a_json_escape_is_masked`, `test_a_parameter_of_a_url_nested_in_another_is_masked_and_its_neighbours_kept`, `test_a_value_ends_where_its_string_or_line_ends`, `test_an_escape_a_url_can_hold_is_part_of_the_value`, `test_a_quote_or_an_encoded_ampersand_a_url_can_hold_is_part_of_the_value`, `test_the_control_ordinary_query_parameters_are_left_alone`, `test_the_control_a_short_value_is_left_alone_and_none_drives_a_refusal` |
| A Stripe secret or restricted key, an npm token or a Hugging Face token is masked and refused | Three vendor shapes join `domain/redaction.py` and `tools/leakscan.py` under the same names, and the redactor counts each as a credential on its own evidence (`HIGH_CONFIDENCE_LABELS`): `stripe-secret-key`, `sk_` or `rk_`, then the mode (`live`, `test` or `prod`; Stripe's documentation names the first two, the gitleaks rule also takes the third) and `_`, then at least 20 letters and digits with no ceiling, since with one, `_TAIL` would leave a longer key unmasked; `npm-token`, `npm_` and exactly 36 letters and digits; `huggingface-token`, `hf_` and exactly 34 letters. A publishable Stripe key (`pk_`) is meant to be shown and is left alone, as is a longer run after `npm_` or `hf_` (`npm_config_cache`, a C or Objective-C name after `hf_`) and an upper-case prefix, which none of the three vendors is documented to issue; so is a placeholder in snake case (`sk_test_your_secret_key_here`, `hf_your_hugging_face_access_token_now`), since none of the three bodies holds `_`. Each begins at `_LEAD` and ends at `_TAIL`, as the other vendor shapes do, behind a lookahead on its first two or three characters rather than its first (`_lead` now takes what follows), because `r`, `s`, `n` and `h` are common letters. So each is masked in a transcript and in what `quoted_value` and `listed_value` show; a skill file holding one is refused (`CredentialInSkill`) before the improver or the judge is called; a replacement that adds one is refused; a configured child environment value holding one is named whatever the variable is called; and the leak scanner reports one in the repository. In an assignment the vendor shape masks the value before the assignment shape reaches it, so `api_key = <key>` reads `api_key = [REDACTED:stripe-secret-key]`, one mask. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both): of 8 samples (`sk_` and `rk_` in each of the three modes, with bodies of 20 to 120 letters and digits, a mixed-case npm token and a Hugging Face token) in prose after a space, `redact`, `quoted_value` and `listed_value` masked 0 (a sample counts as masked when no 12 of its characters in a row are shown, since a cut shows a long value's head), `credential_labels` named 0, so 0 replacements adding one were refused and 0 child environment values holding one were named, the leak scanner reported 0, and a `tdd` skill holding one of the three kinds (one run each) made a candidate, the token in 11 prompts, the improver's and the judge's among them; only an assignment the assignment shape knows (`token=`) masked one. After: 8 of 8 in each, and each such skill is refused with only the scorer called and the token in no prompt. Across the repository's own 161 text files at this change (57,220 lines) neither the redactor nor the scanner finds any of the three. 4 MiB of English prose is masked in 0.67 s on 3.13 and 0.55 s on 3.11, against 0.55 s and 0.47 s before (best of five, on a loaded machine). Not covered by this row: Stripe's organization keys (`sk_org_`), whose format its documentation does not give; a placeholder of the same shape (`hf_` and 34 `x`, or `sk_test_` and a camel-case `YourStripeSecretKeyGoesHere`), which is masked and refused as a key is, as the other vendor shapes treat theirs; the leak scanner's rules, which still begin and end at `\b`, so the scanner misses one followed by `_`, which the redactor masks; and speed, which is measured here and not tested | `domain/redaction.py`, `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_the_samples_are_as_long_as_the_formats_say`, `test_each_is_masked_where_it_stands_and_named_by_its_shape`, `test_an_assignment_holding_one_is_masked_once_by_its_shape`, `test_a_quoted_or_listed_value_holding_one_shows_its_placeholder`, `test_a_replacement_adding_one_is_refused_by_its_shape`, `test_a_configured_child_environment_value_holding_one_is_named`, `test_the_leak_scanner_reports_each_by_its_shape`, `test_a_skill_holding_one_is_refused_before_any_model_role_sees_it`, `test_the_control_what_is_none_of_them_is_left_alone` |
| The parser and the integrity check quote what they found in an edited candidate record, masked, bounded and on one line | The strict parser of a stored validation record (`ValidationRecord.parse` in `domain/validation.py`) and the integrity check run before `review` and `apply` (`verify_full_integrity` in `domain/integrity.py`) name each piece of text they refuse that was read from the record through `quoted_value`, so it is masked, kept on its line and cut at its length, and each list of them through `quoted_values` (new in `domain/redaction.py`), which names the first 5 that way and counts the rest (`and N more`). In the parser: an unknown key, named bare when it reads as a field name and quoting would leave it as it is (so `carries unknown key(s): extra` reads as before), else quoted, the way the config loader names a key it refuses; a digest; a case kind; a case key and what it recomputes to; a judgement schema; a preferred label; a preferred arm with its seed and case key; a count or version below its floor; the record schema; the evidence call count; a duplicate rubric id, ref, outcome or judgement; a case recorded as both kinds; a target case no ref names; a judgement about no outcome; and the missing and unused rubric bindings. In the stored gate's comparison with its recomputation: the value on each side, the recomputed one included, since a case key in it is built from stored text; and a key on one side only, as `path.key` when it is plain, as before, else as `path[...]` with the key quoted. In the integrity check: the recorded patch hash and base hash, by their 12-character prefix as before when each is a digest and quoted when it is not (`_recorded_digest`); the skill file's name and folder, the skill id, the record's candidate id, the case keys claimed and not bound or bound and not claimed, target cases the candidate does not claim or that have no replay, a claimed failure's rubric id and version, a bound rubric's id, and an outcome's case, rubric and criterion. The integrity refusal lists its first 20 problems (`PROBLEMS_SHOWN`) and then `and N more not shown`, its first line keeping the full count. A plain value reads as its `repr` did while that is at most 120 characters, and a plain key of up to 64 is named bare. The numbers the integrity check reads from the record (the patch's added and removed lines, a bound rubric's version) and the parser's preference-ratio bound are printed as before: by then each is a number (the candidate loader reads the counts with `int`, the parser refuses any other type), and the state loader refuses an integer larger than any double, so none holds a line break or a credential. Called directly with an integer of 4,000 digits where it quotes one (for example the evidence call count, a policy count below 0, a rubric binding's version below 1, a claimed failure's rubric version, a value in the stored gate's comparison), either check cuts it; the patch's added and removed lines and a bound rubric's version are printed whole. The state loader refuses such an integer before `review` or `apply` gets that far. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both), with a real validated candidate whose stored record was then edited and `review`, `review --json` and `apply` run on it: an unknown key holding a `ghp_` token, a line break and `config error: forged` printed the token and a second refusal line beginning `config error: forged`, in the record, its gate, the gate's metrics or policy, and a judgement's case (5 places); the same text as the record's digest, candidate id, a case key, a rubric id, a rubric source digest, a fixed case key, the schema, the skill id, or a claimed failure's rubric, criterion or conversation printed the token whole, its line break already escaped by `repr` (11 more); a line break and `config error: forged` before the token, as the recorded patch hash, printed a second line beginning `config erro`, the 12 characters of its prefix keeping the break; a key or a digest of 1,000,000 characters made a refusal of 1,000,578 and 1,000,624 bytes; and 10,000 extra metrics keys made one of 599,359 bytes, 10,000 unknown keys 69,466 and 10,000 extra claimed failures 1,158,857. After, on both: none of the 17 prints the token or a second line, the long key and digest make 732 and 776 bytes, and the three volumes 1,687, 617 and 1,681; `doctor`, which reads none of this, prints the same before and after (899 bytes under the probe's state path). 57 mutants (`quoted_values` unmasked, unbounded or not counting the rest; a key counted plain whatever it holds, whenever the field-name pattern matches it, masked or not, or never; the unknown keys listed raw, named with `str`, or all quoted; a key on one side only always named plain; the problem list unbounded or not counting the rest; a recorded hash always shown by its prefix, or always quoted; and each of the 43 places this row quotes a value or a list shown with `repr` again) are each caught on both by the module of this row. Not covered by this row: the refusal of a candidate whose gate failed or whose skill file no longer matches its base in `app/promote.py` (`GateFailed`, `BaseHashMismatch`), which the row on review and apply quoting the status, the gate reasons and the skill path they refuse covers, with what that row leaves out; a declared id that does not match the file, which the state store names with `repr` (`read_candidate` in `app/state.py`, and the listing `doctor`, `candidates` and the `daily` queue guard print), so a token in it is printed whole on one line (the schema, counts, version and source position it refused the same way are now quoted or named by their field, as the row "The candidate loader quotes the schema it refuses and names a number it cannot read by its field, never by its text" says); what `review` prints of a record that parses (a source's thread id under `--json`, the hypothesis), which a token in still reaches, the mirror paths it prints being those the tree holds, as the row "Review and apply list the mirror copies the tree holds, and the loader reads a record's lists as arrays of strings" says; and a credential of a shape the redaction does not know (`sk_live_`, `npm_` and `hf_` among them), or glued to an ASCII letter or digit before it, which the row on a credential glued to an escape leaves alone, either of which is printed as before | `domain/validation.py`, `domain/integrity.py`, `domain/redaction.py` | `test_each_stored_value_a_parse_refusal_names_is_quoted_masked_on_one_line`, `test_ten_thousand_unknown_keys_are_named_five_and_counted`, `test_a_key_that_is_a_credential_and_nothing_else_is_masked`, `test_a_megabyte_key_or_digest_is_cut`, `test_a_count_or_version_of_four_thousand_digits_is_cut`, `test_a_key_on_one_side_only_is_quoted_and_a_plain_one_named_as_before`, `test_each_stored_value_an_integrity_problem_names_is_quoted`, `test_a_claimed_failure_at_a_version_of_four_thousand_digits_is_cut`, `test_ten_thousand_differences_are_listed_twenty_and_counted`, `test_ten_thousand_claimed_failures_no_ref_binds_are_named_five_and_counted`, `test_review_and_apply_refuse_an_edited_record_without_printing_what_it_holds`, `test_the_control_the_record_as_written_parses_and_verifies`, `test_the_control_a_plain_value_or_key_reads_as_before` |
| Review and apply quote the status, the gate reasons and the skill path they refuse, masked, bounded and on one line | `review` and `apply` (`app/promote.py`) quote what they read from the candidate record when they refuse it. The status a candidate that is not validated is refused for is named through `quoted_value`, so it is masked, kept on its line and cut at its length, and a plain one reads as its `repr` did while that is at most 120 characters (`status is 'gate-failed', not 'validated'`). The skill file's path a base-hash refusal names (the file could not be read, or it now hashes to something else) is named through `named_path` (new in `domain/redaction.py`): bare, as before, when it reads as a path (ASCII letters, digits, the underscore, dot, slash, tilde, plus and hyphen only), is at most 512 characters (`NAMED_PATH_CHARS`) and nothing in it is masked; else quoted, masked and cut at 512. A read that failed is named by the error's number and the system's words for it (`[Errno 2] No such file or directory`), no longer followed by the file name the error repeats, or quoted when it has no number or no words (`_why_unreadable`). A recorded path holding a NUL character, which no file name can, is refused the same way before the read, the path quoted (`holds a NUL character`). `apply` now runs the record-only integrity check before it reads the stored gate, as `review` already did, so a validated record whose stored gate was edited to fail is refused as tampered (`status is 'validated' but the recorded gate did not pass`) before any of its stored reasons is read. The gate-failed refusal that follows names the reasons through `quoted_values`, the first 5 and a count of the rest; no stored record reaches it any more, since that check refuses a validated record whose gate did not pass, and its test stubs the check out. The recorded base hash's 12-character prefix is printed as before: in both commands the integrity check runs before the hash comparison and refuses a record whose base hash differs from its validation's, which the validation parser refuses unless it is a 64-character hex digest. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both apart from the length of a traceback), with a real validated candidate whose stored record was then edited and `review`, `review --json` and `apply` run on it: a status holding a `ghp_` token, a line break and `config error: forged` printed the token, its line break escaped by `repr` (357 bytes from `review`, 166 from `apply`), and a status of 1,000,000 characters made refusals of 1,000,295 and 1,000,104 bytes; with the stored gate edited to fail, `apply` printed a reason holding the token and the line break as written, the token and a second line beginning `config error: forged` (130 bytes), 10,000 reasons of 100 characters made 1,020,067 bytes, a reason that was not a string made `apply` exit 70 with a traceback, and reasons stored as one string were joined a character at a time (250 bytes), while `review` already refused all four as tampered; and with the planted text as the folder in the recorded path (the skill id edited to match) both commands printed the token and a second line, when the file could not be read (533 and 532 bytes) and when a different `SKILL.md` was planted there (462 and 378), with the planted text as the file's name `review` printed both (523; `apply` refuses that name as tampered), and a folder name of 1,000,000 characters made 2,000,404 and 2,000,403 bytes, the path printed twice, once inside the error; and a NUL character in the recorded folder made both commands exit 70 with a traceback (`ValueError: embedded null byte`), as one after the file's name did `review` (`apply` refuses that name as tampered, in 591 bytes before and after). After, on both: none of these prints the token, a second line or a traceback; the status refusals are 340 and 149 bytes and those of the long status 447 and 256; `apply` refuses each of the four edited gates as tampered in 610 to 652 bytes; the planted folder makes 427 and 426 bytes, 448 and 364 with a file planted in it, the planted file name 422 from `review`, the long folder name 894 and 893, and the NUL in the folder 398 and 397 and after the name 398 from `review`. Unchanged before and after: a stored gate replaced by a list or a string is refused by the candidate loader (244 and 243 bytes), a base hash edited to zeros is refused as tampered (729 and 728), and `doctor` prints the same in every case (899 bytes under the probe's state path, 1,235 and 1,234 where the loader refuses the record). 33 mutants (`named_path` always bare, always quoted, without the mask check, without the length check, matching only a prefix, cutting at the 120 characters of a quoted value, cutting before it masks, or with a length of 1,024 or 511; the plain-path pattern widened to any line, to a space, or to the other characters Python breaks a line at; the status named with `repr` in `review` or in `apply`; the first integrity call of `apply` removed, moved after the gate is read, run as `review`, or run without the configured rubrics; the reasons joined raw; the path named raw in either hash refusal or in the read refusal; the read error shown with `str`; `_why_unreadable` unquoted in either branch, always numbered, or numbering an error the system has no words for; and the NUL refusal removed, naming the path raw, naming `review` as `apply`, raised as `GateFailed`, without its hint, or looking for the NUL in the first 512 characters only) are each caught on both by the module of this row. Not covered by this row: every refusal of the skill store in `adapters/skill_fs.py` that names the recorded path, which it names as written or as resolved, unmasked, so a token in it is printed whole (when the file is read or judged as a target: a link, not a regular file, not UTF-8, a mirror copy, a skill with no canonical copy, or outside every configured root, among others; in the write `apply` makes, `write_canonical`: a link below the skill root, a protected path, a file missing or changed between the read and the write, a backup or post-write check that fails, or a write or a parent folder that fails, among others); `rollback`, whose refusals name the candidate id, and the backup-manifest checks it reaches in `adapters/skill_fs.py`; the candidate id each refusal names, read from the record, which the loader requires to match the name of its file; the candidate loader's own refusals, which the row on the parser and the integrity check quoting what they found in an edited candidate record lists; what `review` prints of a record that parses (a source's thread id under `--json`, the hypothesis), which a token in still reaches, the mirror paths it prints being those the tree holds, as the row "Review and apply list the mirror copies the tree holds, and the loader reads a record's lists as arrays of strings" says; the file `review` reads at the recorded path before the integrity check runs, which it hashes and does not print; and a credential of a shape the redaction does not know (`sk_live_`, `npm_` and `hf_` among them), or glued to an ASCII letter or digit before it, which is printed as before (a path holding only such a credential is named bare) | `app/promote.py`, `domain/redaction.py` | `test_a_status_holding_a_token_and_a_line_break_is_quoted_by_review_and_apply`, `test_a_status_of_a_megabyte_is_cut`, `test_a_plain_status_reads_as_before`, `test_apply_names_a_validated_record_whose_gate_was_edited_to_fail_as_tampered`, `test_the_reasons_of_a_failed_gate_are_quoted_masked_and_counted`, `test_a_failed_gate_with_no_reasons_says_so`, `test_a_planted_folder_that_cannot_be_read_is_quoted`, `test_a_planted_folder_holding_another_skill_file_is_quoted`, `test_a_folder_name_of_a_megabyte_is_cut`, `test_a_path_holding_a_nul_is_refused_not_fallen_over`, `test_a_plain_path_is_named_bare_as_before`, `test_a_plain_path_is_itself`, `test_a_path_one_character_too_long_is_quoted_and_cut`, `test_a_credential_across_the_cut_is_masked_before_the_cut`, `test_a_path_that_is_a_credential_and_nothing_else_is_masked`, `test_a_path_holding_anything_a_path_need_not_hold_is_quoted`, `test_an_os_error_is_named_by_its_number_and_words_without_its_file_name`, `test_an_os_error_with_no_number_is_quoted` |
| Review asks of its target the questions the write asks before it opens the file, and review and apply refuse a skill listed as protected | `review` (`cli.py`) asks of the candidate's target, before it prints the report or a token, the questions the write asks of it before it opens the file, by the method the write asks them with: `assert_write_target`, new in `adapters/skill_fs.py`, which `write_canonical` now calls in place of its own checks and `apply` (`app/promote.py`) calls before it makes the backup directory. In its order: a symbolic link below the configured root on the path as spelled, or a spelling outside every root that resolves into a canonical one (`LinkedTargetRefused`); a file that is not `SKILL.md` (`NonDocumentWriteRefused`); a file inside a protected path (`SelfMutationRefused`); a mirror copy, a copy of a skill with no canonical copy, or no skill document in any configured root (`MirrorEditRefused`); and, new, a skill listed in `[guard].protected_skill_ids` (`SelfMutationRefused`), a list that was read only when a proposal is made and that `build_skill_store` (`app/wiring.py`) now hands to the skill store. That refusal judges both the skill the inventory finds at the target and the skill the record names, and names the path through `named_path` and the skill through `quoted_value` (`skill 'tdd' is listed in [guard].protected_skill_ids`). The words say which command refuses: `refusing to review` from `review`; from `apply` and the write, `refusing to replace` for a link and `refusing to write` for the others, as the write said before. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both), with a real validated candidate: with its skill listed in `[guard].protected_skill_ids` after it was validated, `review` printed a token and an apply command (2,543 bytes) and `apply` wrote the skill (661 bytes, exit 0); with the record's path spelled through a link (a skill folder that is a link below its root, a folder outside every root that links into one, an alias of the root; the skill id and the diff's labels edited to match), `review` printed a token and an apply command (2,535, 2,508 and 2,510 bytes) that `apply` refused with `LinkedTargetRefused` (350, 371 and 371 bytes) after it had made a backup directory, which it left empty; and with the path relabelled to a copy of the skill inside a protected path (the rubrics directory), `review` refused it as an unrecognised target (`MirrorEditRefused`, 264 bytes) and `apply` as protected (`SelfMutationRefused`, 317 bytes), again after making a backup directory it left empty. After, on both: `review` refuses the five with exit 3 and no token (340 bytes for the listed skill; 349, 370 and 370 for the links; 318, as protected, for the copy), and `apply` refuses the five (339; 350, 371 and 371; 317) without making a backup directory; a clean candidate is reviewed with a token (2,543 bytes) and applied (661), with one backup directory, as before. 22 mutants (the protected-skill check removed, or judging only the skill the inventory finds, or only the one the record names; the skill store not keeping the list, or `build_skill_store` not handing it over; `review` asking only the canonical-copy question, as before, or asking nothing; the link refusal saying `replace` from `review`, or `write` from the write; the other refusals saying `write` from `review`; the link check, the document check, the protected-path check or the canonical-copy check dropped from the method; the protected-path check made after the canonical-copy check; the target left unresolved; `write_canonical` no longer asking the method, or asking it without the skill the record names; `apply` not asking it before the backup directory, or asking it as `review`; and the path or the skill named raw in the new refusal) are each caught on both, by the module of this row apart from two that the tests the write already had catch (the document check and the target left unresolved, in `tests/test_skill_identity_and_payloads.py`). Not covered by this row: `rollback`, which does not read `[guard].protected_skill_ids` and asks its own questions of the target, so a skill listed after its apply is still restored; an apply that finishes an earlier run of itself (the skill already holds the candidate's text and its newest backup manifest records that write), which records the write without asking the method, since it writes nothing to the skill; an apply refused by the write after the target was judged (the file gone or changed since the read, a link swapped in below the root after the walk, a backup, a write or the check after the write that fails), which still leaves the backup directory it made; the file `review` and `apply` read at the recorded path before they judge the target, which they read through a link above the document and hash, printing nothing of it; the skill id the record names, judged on its own only by the store-level tests, since from the command line it names the folder the inventory names the skill by (the integrity check requires it) unless the path is reached through a link, which is refused first; the other refusals of the method, which name the path as written or as resolved, unmasked, as the row "Review and apply quote the status, the gate reasons and the skill path they refuse, masked, bounded and on one line" lists; and what `review` prints of a record that passes (a source's thread id under `--json`, the hypothesis), unmasked; the mirror paths a candidate records, which neither the token nor the integrity check binds, are no longer what `review` prints or `apply` records, as the row "Review and apply list the mirror copies the tree holds, and the loader reads a record's lists as arrays of strings" says | `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `app/promote.py`, `app/wiring.py`, `cli.py` | `test_a_skill_listed_as_protected_after_validation_gets_no_token_and_is_not_written`, `test_a_skill_not_listed_is_reviewed_as_before`, `test_a_skill_folder_that_is_a_link_below_its_root_gets_no_token`, `test_a_folder_outside_every_root_that_links_into_one_gets_no_token`, `test_an_alias_of_the_root_gets_no_token`, `test_a_copy_of_the_skill_inside_a_protected_path_is_refused_as_protected_by_both`, `test_the_skill_the_inventory_finds_is_judged`, `test_the_skill_the_record_names_is_judged`, `test_the_write_itself_refuses_the_skill_the_record_names_and_makes_no_backup`, `test_a_skill_path_that_does_not_read_as_one_is_named_quoted_on_one_line`, `test_an_unlisted_skill_is_the_resolved_target`, `test_a_clean_candidate_is_reviewed_with_a_token_and_applied` |
| Review and apply list the mirror copies the tree holds, and the loader reads a record's lists as arrays of strings | `review` (`cli.py`) lists under `MIRROR COPIES (not written by apply)`, and `review --json` under `mirror_paths`, the mirror copies the live inventory holds of the skill it reviews, and `apply` (`app/promote.py`) records those in its receipt as `mirror_paths_not_written`, both from `mirror_paths_of`, new in `adapters/skill_fs.py`, asked after the target is judged (the run that finishes an earlier apply judges no target, and asks it before it records the write); the list the candidate record carries (`target.mirror_paths`), which neither the approval token nor the integrity check binds, is no longer read by either. An apply that finishes an earlier run of itself thus records the copies the tree holds when it runs. The text names each path through `named_path`: bare when it reads as a path, else quoted, masked and cut, so a mirror folder named with a token or a line break is printed masked and on its line. The candidate loader (`domain/candidate.py`) now reads `target.mirror_paths`, `risks` and `notes` as arrays of strings (`_strings`): a field that is not an array is refused by its name and type (`target.mirror_paths must be an array of strings, got str`), an item that is not a string by its position and type (`risks[1] must be a string, got dict`), the value never quoted, so `review` and `apply` refuse the record as `StateUnreadable`. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both; the byte counts are those under the probe's temporary directory, so each path printed adds to them as that directory's path grows), with a real validated candidate whose record was then edited and `review`, `review --json` and `apply` run on it: a clean candidate was reviewed in 2,543 and 8,406 bytes and applied in 661, its receipt listing its one mirror; a path holding a `ghp_` token added to the list was printed whole by both reviews (2,602 and 8,468 bytes) and recorded in the receipt; a path holding a line break was printed with the break spelled (2,593) and recorded; a path of 1,000,000 characters made reviews of 1,002,488 and 1,008,351 bytes; 10,000 paths made 241,359 and 277,219 bytes and a receipt listing 10,000; an emptied list dropped the section (2,430 bytes) and the receipt listed no mirror; a list that was one string of 8 characters was printed and recorded as 8 paths of one character (2,517), and items that were an object and a number were printed as their `str` (2,498); a list that was a number was already refused by the loader (228 bytes); with the record left alone and the tree changed, a mirror root added since the proposal was neither listed nor recorded, and a mirror copy removed since was still listed and recorded; and `risks` or `notes` edited to one string or to items that are not strings were reviewed (2,422 to 2,567 bytes) and applied. After, on both: the token, line-break, megabyte, ten-thousand and emptied lists are reviewed in 2,543 and 8,406 bytes and applied in 661 as the clean candidate is, the receipt listing the one mirror the tree holds; the one-string, number and not-strings lists are refused by the loader in 257, 257 and 250 bytes, and the four `risks` and `notes` edits in 236 to 243, by both reviews and `apply`; the added root is listed (2,618 and 8,484 bytes) and recorded, the removed copy is neither (2,430 and 8,327, the receipt listing no mirror); and a mirror root added with a token in its folder name is printed with the token masked (2,631 bytes), one with a line break in its folder name with the break spelled inside the quoted path (2,646). 19 mutants (the loader reading `target.mirror_paths`, `risks` or `notes` as before; `_strings` without its array check, accepting a string or an object, without its item check, naming the value or the item rather than its type, or naming the position one past it; `mirror_paths_of` giving the copies of the skill it finds at a mirror copy, judging the path unresolved, or giving the first copy only; `review` showing the record's list, or reading it as before; the text naming the paths raw; and `apply` recording the record's list in its first run, in the run that finishes an earlier one, or in the receipt it writes) are each caught on both by the module of this row. Not covered by this row: `review --json`, which lists the copies the tree holds as they are on disk, unmasked, as it names the skill's own path, so a token in a mirror folder's name is printed whole there; the text, whose list of the copies the tree holds is not bounded; the list the record carries, which `daily` still writes and the record keeps, and which the integrity check still reads only to refuse a record that lists its own skill file as a mirror; the other fields the loader reads with `str()` (the hypothesis, the intended change, the rollback note, the status, and the target's ids and path, among others), so one that is not a string is read as its `str`; and a credential of a shape the redaction does not know, which a mirror folder's name still carries into the text whole | `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `app/promote.py`, `cli.py`, `domain/candidate.py` | `test_the_control_a_clean_candidate_lists_its_one_mirror_and_records_it`, `test_a_token_and_a_line_break_planted_in_the_list_are_neither_printed_nor_recorded`, `test_a_list_of_a_megabyte_or_of_ten_thousand_paths_is_neither_printed_nor_recorded`, `test_an_emptied_list_does_not_hide_the_mirror_the_tree_holds`, `test_a_mirror_added_since_the_proposal_is_listed_and_recorded`, `test_a_mirror_removed_since_the_proposal_is_neither_listed_nor_recorded`, `test_a_mirror_path_holding_a_token_or_a_line_break_is_quoted_masked_on_one_line`, `test_an_apply_finished_by_a_second_run_records_the_mirror_the_tree_holds`, `test_review_and_apply_refuse_a_list_that_is_one_string_by_its_name`, `test_a_field_that_is_not_an_array_is_refused_by_its_name_and_type`, `test_an_item_that_is_not_a_string_is_refused_by_its_position_and_type`, `test_the_control_arrays_of_strings_and_absent_fields_read_as_before`, `test_only_a_canonical_copy_has_mirrors_and_it_is_judged_resolved` |
| The candidate loader quotes the schema it refuses and names a number it cannot read by its field, never by its text | The candidate loader (`Candidate.from_dict` in `domain/candidate.py`) names a schema it refuses through `quoted_value`, so it is masked, kept on its line and cut at 120 characters, and a plain one reads as its `repr` did (`unexpected candidate schema 'asi.candidate.v0'`); and it reads `patch.added_lines`, `patch.removed_lines`, each rubric failure's `rubric_version` and each source's `position` through `_integer` (new), which takes what `int()` took and refuses the rest by the field, its index and the type found (`sources[0].position must be an integer, got str`), never by the text, where the refusal of `int()` quoted up to 200 characters of it. `review` and `apply` refuse such a record as `StateUnreadable`, and `candidates` and `doctor` list it as unreadable with that reason, as before. After, on both: none of the six prints the token or a second line; the token schema makes 276, 276, 344, 488, 1,267 and about 5,730 bytes, the schema of a megabyte 383, 383, 451, 594, 1,374 and about 5,950, the object 261, and the three number fields 246, 248 and 262 from `review`; an unedited record is reviewed (2,543 bytes) and applied (661) as before. 18 mutants (the schema named with `repr` as before, or quoted without a bound; `patch.added_lines`, `patch.removed_lines`, the rubric version or the source position read with `int()` as before; the removed lines named as the added lines; the rubric failure's or the source's index always 0; `_integer` naming the value rather than its type, letting a `TypeError` or a `ValueError` through unnamed, or refusing what `int()` took, for every field or for the removed lines or the rubric version alone; and the default of an absent count made 1, for either, or of an absent rubric version made 0) are each caught on both by the module of this row. | The candidate loader keeps the improver's prose and the notes masked when it reads a record | The candidate loader (`Candidate.from_dict` in `domain/candidate.py`) reads the hypothesis, the intended change and the rollback note with `str()`, as before, and then through `kept_prose`, so each is masked with the transcript's shapes; and it reads each risk and each note, once `_strings` has refused a list that is not an array of strings as before, through `kept_prose` the same way. That is how the judgement reader in `domain/validation.py` already read the judge's rationale. The proposal parser (`parse_proposal`) masks the hypothesis, the intended change, the risks and the rollback note when the tool writes them, so a record this tool wrote reads back as written, with two exceptions: masking prose already masked changes it only where two masks a URL parameter's shape joins become one (measured on 200,000 strings composed of credentials, masks and glue: 963 changed, each only by fewer masks over the same other text, and none changed again on a third pass; the same 963 when each masked text is stripped, as the proposal parser strips it), and a note, which the tool writes unmasked, changes only where it holds a credential's shape. The loader clips, strips, drops and refuses nothing it did not before. The module of this row reads each of the five fields over empty, blank, plain and long (100,040 characters) text; text with precomposed and decomposed accents, an astral character and a lone surrogate; text holding C0 and C1 controls, DEL, a zero-width space, a bidi override and a line separator; text that masking twice would change; one example of each of the fifteen shapes `redact` knows, two of them across a line break; and the shortest text each of six shapes masks (12 to 20 characters; an assignment's value is six characters at least, so no shape masks a text shorter than 12); and the notes `apply` (a finishing run's among them), `rollback` and a blocked run write, two of them holding a credential where a path or an operator's name goes; and it compares what the loader keeps with what `redact` alone makes of it. A test checks that the examples cover the shapes `domain/redaction.py` lists, that the shortest of them is 12 characters, and that the text masking twice would change still does; and a record in each of the six statuses `domain/candidate.py` defines is read masked. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both; the byte counts are those under the probe's temporary directory, whose path the report names), with a real validated candidate whose record was then edited: a `ghp_` token appended to the hypothesis, the intended change or the rollback note, or added as a last risk or a last note, was printed whole by `review` (2,588, 2,588, 2,588, 2,594 and 2,595 bytes, in that order) and by `review --json` (8,451, 8,451, 8,451, 8,460 and 8,461), each exiting 0; and `apply` of a record so edited in the hypothesis, or in a note, exited 0 and left the token in the record it closed as applied. and an unedited record is reviewed as before, in text (2,543 bytes) and in JSON (8,406), byte for byte once the temporary directory's path is set aside. `rollback` and the blocked-run close in `app/daily.py` build the record they write from the one this loader read, so they keep the mask too; neither is measured or tested by this row. 50 mutants (the hypothesis, the intended change, the rollback note, the risks or the notes read as before; only the first risk, or only the last note, masked; the hypothesis, or a note, left as read when it already holds a mask, or when it is not ASCII; the hypothesis normalised to NFC; the intended change masked twice; the risks re-encoded with replacement characters; the escape character dropped from a note, the NUL character from the rollback note, or each character that does not print, other than tab, newline and carriage return, from the hypothesis; the hypothesis, or a risk, masked only when it holds `ghp_` or `sk-`;
