| The candidate listing masks what it reads from a record and cuts what no real one could be | `candidates` lists records that no integrity check has read, so it now shows each one's status, skill id, canonical path and creation time, in the text and in `--json` alike, through `listed_value` (new in `domain/redaction.py`): masked with the shapes `redact` knows, then cut, the cut saying how many characters it left out (`... [N more chars not shown]`); the limit and the count are in characters, not bytes. Unlike `quoted_value` it adds no quotes, so a value that holds no credential shape and is no longer than its limit lists exactly as before, a skill id with spaces or letters outside ASCII included. The limits: 255 characters for the skill id (`LISTED_SKILL_ID_CHARS`; a skill id is a directory name, at most 255 bytes on APFS and ext4 and so at most 255 characters), 4,096 for the path (`LISTED_PATH_CHARS`; `PATH_MAX`, the longest path a system call takes by name, is 1,024 bytes on macOS and 4,096 on Linux), and 120 for the status and the creation time (`QUOTED_VALUE_CHARS`). The text line is built from the row the JSON carries, and `_emit` keeps it on one line as before. Measured before (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both), with a real validated candidate whose record was then edited and `candidates`, `candidates --all`, `candidates --json` and `candidates --all --json` run on it (the byte counts are those under the probe's temporary directory: the JSON listings name the canonical path, which holds that directory's path unless the case edits it, and the text listings name no path): a `ghp_` token followed by a line break and `config error: forged` in the skill id was printed whole by all four (164, 159, 480 and 479 bytes), in the status by the two under `--all` (180 and 469), and in the canonical path or the creation time by the two JSON listings (419 and 418; 459 and 458); the line break was already spelled `\n` in the text and escaped in the JSON, so no line was forged. A value of 1,000,000 characters in the skill id made 1,000,098, 1,000,093, 1,000,414 and 1,000,413 bytes, in the status 1,000,118 and 1,000,407 under `--all`, in the path 1,000,357 and 1,000,356 from the JSON listings, and in the creation time 1,000,397 and 1,000,396. After, on both: none of the four prints the token; the megabyte skill id makes 386, 381, 702 and 701 bytes, the status 271 and 560, the path 4,474 and 4,473, and the creation time 550 and 549; an unedited record lists as before, byte for byte once the temporary directory's path and the run's own creation time are masked (126, 121, 417 and 416 bytes), and so does one whose skill id is `my skill ü-日本` (131, 126, 432 and 431). `doctor` prints 899 bytes in every one of these cases, before and after (it names the temporary directory twice). 31 mutants (`listed_value` not masking, cutting before it masks, never cutting, cutting a value exactly at its limit, judging the length as written rather than as masked, turning a tab into a space, counting the whole length or giving no count in its cut, quoting as `quoted_value` does, stripping, defaulting to the path's limit, cutting and counting by UTF-8 bytes, folding `\r\n` to `\n` before it masks, or cutting a value of over 100,000 characters before it masks; the skill id's limit at 254 or 120 and the path's at 1,024 or 4,095; each of the four fields listed as before; the skill id cut at the default limit, the path at the skill id's, the status at the path's and the creation time at the skill id's; the text line's status or skill id taken from the record rather than the row; the status or the skill id listed as before under `--all` only; and `_emit` no longer keeping a line on its line) are each caught on both by the module of this row. Not covered by this row: the record's id, listed as written, since it is the name the operator types to `review` it and the loader holds it to the candidate id's alphabet and 204 characters, so it holds no line break and no more than that, but a credential spelled in that alphabet after `CAND-` is printed whole; a credential of a shape the redaction does not know, or glued to an ASCII letter or digit before it, which the listing prints as before, as the row "The parser and the integrity check quote what they found in an edited candidate record, masked, bounded and on one line" says of the redaction; the counts of added and removed lines, which the loader reads as `int()` reads them, so a count given as text of up to 4,300 digits is listed whole (4,425 bytes from `candidates`, measured on both) and one of 4,301 makes the record unreadable; the `UNREADABLE` line's file name and reason, which the state store builds; what `review` prints, which the integrity check reads first; and `daily`, which reads a record's skill id as written only to count the open candidates for each skill, and names only the skill ids its own inventory holds | `domain/redaction.py`, `cli.py` | `test_the_listing_text_set_holds_every_shape_redact_knows`, `test_each_kind_of_text_is_shown_as_redact_masks_it_then_cut`, `test_no_shape_survives_and_a_key_the_cut_would_split_is_masked_whole`, `test_a_value_longer_than_its_limit_only_until_masked_is_shown_whole`, `test_a_value_at_the_limit_is_whole_and_one_past_it_is_cut_with_its_count`, `test_a_plain_value_is_shown_exactly_as_written_with_no_quotes`, `test_the_limits_reach_what_this_platform_allows_a_name_and_a_path`, `test_a_credential_in_the_skill_id_path_or_creation_time_is_masked_when_listed`, `test_a_credential_in_the_status_is_masked_when_all_records_are_listed`, `test_a_megabyte_in_each_listed_field_is_cut`, `test_each_field_is_whole_at_its_limit_and_cut_one_past_it`, `test_each_field_too_long_only_until_masked_is_listed_whole`, `test_a_line_break_in_a_listed_field_stays_on_its_line`, `test_the_control_an_unedited_record_and_a_real_odd_name_list_as_written` |

| An export's thread id is held to the limit a listed id is held to | `_scan` in `adapters/fixture_source.py` passes over an export whose thread id is longer than `MAX_THREAD_ID_LENGTH` (256 characters, in `domain/discovery.py`), the limit the command-line source already holds a listed id to at discovery (the row "A listed entry that cannot be taken as a thread is named on the receipt, not only dropped"); its reason gives the id's length and the limit and quotes the id through `quoted_value` (masked, then cut at 120), `doctor` warns for it, and the exports beside it are read as before. | A long workspace is a bounded selection bucket key | `_bucket_of` in `app/daily.py` passes the workspace through `bucket_key` in `domain/selection.py` when the fairness dimension is the workspace: a key of up to 512 characters (`BUCKET_KEY_CHARS`) is kept as written, neither trimmed nor case-folded, so two workspaces that differ only in case or surrounding spaces are two buckets, and a longer one is cut there and fingerprinted, "... [N characters, sha256 <first 16 hex of the whole value>]", so two workspaces sharing their first 512 characters are still two buckets, one workspace is one bucket however many threads name it, and a fingerprinted key, longer than the limit, never equals a key kept whole; a lone surrogate is hashed rather than refused. The selection report writes each key three times into the receipt (`buckets`, `selected_per_bucket`, `exhausted_buckets`), and the workspace is the export's `workspacePath` or a listing's `Workspace:` line, untrusted and unbounded. | A replacement is diffed with the line endings apply writes | The store reads a skill document with its line endings folded to LF (`read_text` in `adapters/skill_fs.py`), and `write_canonical` writes the replacement folded the same way, in the document's own convention (the row "A document is one text under one identity from proposal to rollback, whatever its line endings"); the improver's replacement was kept with the line endings it was sent with, and checked, diffed against the folded base and replayed so. `propose_candidate` in `app/pipeline.py` now folds it (`normalize_text`) before any of that, so the candidate holds, and `review` diffs, the text apply writes, and a reply in CRLF makes the same candidate as one in LF. A reply that changes only the line endings was a no-op before and still is, its reason now adding that the patch has no unified diff. 7 mutants (the replacement not folded; only CRLF folded; a lone carriage return dropped, or made a space; the replacement folded to CRLF; only the diff given the folded text; the replacement folded after its checks) are each caught on both. | The recovery command must not destroy work made after the apply | Rollback overwrites only the text its own apply wrote (`new_sha256` must match the live file); a file already holding the previous text is a verified no-op; a later edit is a `BaseHashMismatch` refusal; a symlink at the recorded path is refused before any hash is read, and the restore is an atomic replace rather than a copy through the path | `adapters/skill_fs.py`, `jsonio.py` | `test_a_rollback_refuses_to_overwrite_edits_made_after_the_apply`, `test_rolling_back_twice_is_a_verified_no_op`, `test_a_manifest_missing_the_applied_hash_is_refused_by_name`, `test_a_target_that_became_a_symlink_is_refused_and_the_link_target_untouched`, `test_a_manifest_without_a_recorded_mode_restores_at_the_live_files_mode` |
| The recovery command must find only its own apply | A backup directory is named `<candidate id>--<stamp>`, and a candidate id may itself contain `--`. `latest_backup_manifest` reads the owner off the directory name by splitting at the last `--`, never by string prefix (measured before this row, `CAND-a` was handed the backup of `CAND-a--b`), and `backup_dir_for` refuses a stamp carrying `--` so the split is always unambiguous | `app/state.py` | `test_a_backup_belongs_to_exactly_the_candidate_whose_id_names_it`, `test_a_stamp_that_would_blur_the_owner_is_refused_at_write_time`, `test_the_latest_backup_is_the_newest_stamp_for_that_candidate` |

## The daily path cannot mutate anything

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Prove it structurally, not by inspection | An AST test asserts `app/daily.py` does not import the promotion module and never calls the write method | `app/daily.py` | `test_the_daily_module_does_not_import_the_promotion_module`, `test_the_daily_module_never_calls_the_write_method` |
| Prove it observationally too | A full pass leaves the skill tree digest unchanged and no file content altered | `app/daily.py` | `test_the_skill_tree_digest_is_unchanged_by_a_full_pass`, `test_no_skill_file_content_changes_on_disk` |
| A dry run must persist nothing | `daily` without `--commit` writes no artifact under the state directory: the store withholds every record write in dry-run mode and records what it withheld, measured on the whole tree rather than one directory (an earlier version checked only the candidate directory while conversations, scorecards and quarantine records were being written). What a dry run leaves is the owner-only layout, the run lock and, on a fresh tree, the layout's README.txt, a fixed text that the layout writes whenever it is absent, the one file the test of a dry run from an empty state allows. Measured (3.11 and 3.13, the same on both), after a scout found the operations guide naming only the layout and the lock: a dry run on a fresh tree added the 13 state directories, the lock and README.txt (mode 0600, the same bytes on both) and nothing else; after a committed day, a dry run of the same day changed the lock's bytes alone, and one of the next day the lock's bytes and the modification time of the directory each model call's scratch is made and removed in; nothing outside the state directory changed in any of the three. The guide now names README.txt | `app/state.py`, `app/daily.py`, `cli.py` | `test_04_a_dry_run_persists_nothing`, `test_a_dry_run_from_an_empty_state_writes_no_artifact`, `test_a_dry_run_leaves_existing_state_byte_identical`, `test_the_raw_export_write_is_withheld_too` |
| A dry run may change existing state only by resetting a drifted mode | `ensure()`, which every `daily` run calls before anything else, resets any existing state file or directory whose mode is not owner-only and records which, directories included and a reset the store already made while a subdirectory was being opened for wiring; the receipt counts them (`state_modes_repaired`) and names them, the dry-run note says content is untouched, and the snapshot the dry-run tests compare includes each file's mode so a chmod cannot hide behind byte-identical content | `app/state.py`, `app/daily.py` | `test_a_dry_run_still_resets_a_drifted_mode_without_touching_the_bytes`, `test_a_reset_made_by_dir_before_the_walk_is_still_reported_once`, `test_a_directory_dir_creates_is_not_reported_as_a_repair` |
| A dry run must preview the verdict a commit would reach | Deferral attempts are counted before the write, so the cap decision is identical whether or not the write lands | `app/daily.py` | `test_a_dry_run_reaches_the_defer_verdict_a_commit_would` |
| A dry run must report the backlog on disk, not the proposals it withheld | `open_candidates_after` counts open-status candidate files on disk, so a dry run's own proposals never inflate it; `candidates_proposed` keeps the work the run did, and each candidate's `persisted` flag says whether its file reached disk | `app/daily.py`, `app/receipt.py`, `app/state.py` | `test_a_dry_run_reports_the_backlog_on_disk_not_the_proposals_it_withheld` |
| Promotion must never run through a withholding store | A store in dry-run mode refuses to write a promotion receipt or create a backup directory, so an apply cannot keep the edit and drop its own accounting | `app/state.py` | `test_promotion_refuses_a_store_that_withholds_writes` |
| The receipt legend may drift from the receipt | Every count key a run emits must be in the `counts` block of `docs/OPERATIONS.md` and vice versa, with an extractor control | `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, `app/receipt.py` | `test_every_emitted_count_is_documented_and_every_documented_count_is_emitted`, `test_the_legend_extractor_finds_keys_and_skips_prose_and_continuations` |

## Repeat runs, locks and determinism

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A same-day re-run must not re-score | The day key is the idempotence key | `app/daily.py` | `test_re_running_the_same_day_does_not_re_score`, `test_a_different_day_is_processed_again` |
| Two runs must not overlap | An exclusive lock is held for the pass and released even when the body raises | `app/lock.py` | `test_two_runs_cannot_hold_the_lock_at_once`, `test_the_lock_is_released_after_the_block`, `test_the_lock_is_released_even_when_the_body_raises` |
| Output must be reproducible | Two identical runs produce identical receipts | `app/receipt.py` | `test_two_identical_runs_produce_identical_receipts` |

## Public safety

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| No private content, ever | The scanner covers tracked content, tracked paths, full history and commit metadata; its banned literals are assembled from fragments so its own source is scanned like any other file | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_a_no_tracked_file_content_matches_any_rule`, `test_b_no_tracked_path_names_a_private_artifact`, `test_c_history_contains_no_banned_literal` |
| The scanner must be two-sided | Every rule fires on its own positive sample and stays silent on its negative one | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_a_every_rule_is_two_sided`, `test_c_the_scanner_fires_on_a_planted_absolute_home_path`, `test_e_the_scanner_stays_silent_on_ordinary_prose` |
| A word-boundary rule is fail-open for a name that appears inside identifiers | The product rule is bounded by letters rather than word characters, because an underscore is a word character and `\b` was silent on the module and filename spellings this tree actually used; the camel-case half is narrowed so it cannot fire on the AWS access-key-id prefix that really occurs here | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_d2_the_scanner_fires_on_the_internal_agent_product_name`, `test_d3_the_product_rule_respects_word_boundaries`, `test_d3b_the_product_rule_fires_on_the_identifier_form`, `test_d3c_the_product_rule_stays_silent_on_the_aws_key_prefix` |
| The whole-tree claim must be checkable by hand | A case-insensitive search over every tracked path and body returns zero occurrences of the product name and of the sampled title, using a pattern that is a strict superset of the word-boundary search a reviewer would run | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_d6_no_tracked_content_names_the_internal_product_or_sampled_title`, `test_d7_that_search_really_would_find_something` |
| No exemptions may accumulate quietly | The allowance mechanism exists, is printed in every report, and is empty | `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_f_allowances_are_empty` |
| Fixtures must be synthetic and deterministic | The committed fixtures match their builder byte for byte, and no fixture matches any scanner rule | `examples/build_fixtures.py` | `test_a_committed_fixtures_match_the_builder_byte_for_byte`, `test_e_no_fixture_contains_a_banned_literal` |
| A public flip must need no cleanup | MIT licensed, no unpublishable classifier, no absolute home paths, empty runtime dependencies; a published clone may have remotes, but every fetch/push URL must be credential-free HTTPS or git-only SSH to a public host | `pyproject.toml`, `LICENSE`, `tools/leakscan.py` | `test_a_no_unpublishable_classifier`, `test_e_no_absolute_home_path_in_packaging_or_config`, `test_c_runtime_dependencies_are_empty`, `test_a_configured_remotes_are_public_safe`, `test_a2_remote_policy_accepts_credential_free_https_and_ssh`, `test_a3_remote_policy_refuses_credentials_and_local_paths` |
| Documented commands must be real | Every `asi` command line in the docs is parsed against the real argument parser | `cli.py` | `test_b_every_fenced_command_line_parses_completely`, `test_a_inline_flags_in_docs_are_real` |
| Documented refusals must be the ones the CLI prints | Every operator-facing refusal class (`SafetyRefusal` descendants and `LockHeld`) that the code raises is explained in `docs/OPERATIONS.md`, every refusal name the document cites exists in `errors.py`, and the lock path in the docs is the one the code opens; six raised names were missing when this was first measured | `errors.py`, `docs/OPERATIONS.md` | `test_every_raised_operator_facing_refusal_is_documented`, `test_every_documented_refusal_name_is_a_real_error_class`, `test_the_lock_path_in_the_docs_is_the_one_the_code_uses`, `test_controls_for_the_extractors` |
| A crash must not borrow the exit code of a negative answer | An exception no command anticipated is caught last in `main`, named on stderr with its traceback, and exits 70 (`EX_SOFTWARE`), so a scheduler reading 1 always reads a negative answer the tool gave; every declared exit code is distinct and every one, plus argparse's 2, has a row in the OPERATIONS table | `cli.py` | `test_an_unanticipated_exception_is_not_reported_as_a_negative_answer`, `test_the_control_a_refusal_still_exits_3_without_a_traceback`, `test_every_declared_code_is_distinct`, `test_the_exit_code_table_documents_every_declared_code_and_argparse` |
| Every state directory the docs name must exist, and every content-bearing one must have a privacy row | every state subdirectory path cited anywhere in the docs is checked against `StateStore.SUBDIRS`, and every subdirectory except the lock directory (exempted with its reason: the lock holds only a pid) must appear in the asset table of the privacy document, so a reader deciding what is safe to paste has a row for candidates, diagnostics, checkpoints, backups and sandbox scratch, not only for transcripts; the two scheduler log files the packaging templates redirect into the state tree have their own asset row and a mention in the scheduling doc, with the log names read off the templates and pinned to the two expected, so a renamed or added log fails the test | `app/state.py`, `docs/PRIVACY_AND_THREAT_MODEL.md`, `packaging/` | `test_every_documented_state_directory_exists`, `test_every_content_bearing_directory_has_a_privacy_row`, `test_the_extractor_sees_the_known_rows`, `test_every_template_log_has_a_privacy_row_and_a_scheduling_mention` |

## The map is checked against the suite

A table of claims about tests is not evidence, and it decays silently: a renamed
test leaves the row reading exactly as it did when it was true. So the map is
verified mechanically, and the verifier is two-sided, because a checker that
found no references at all would also report that every reference resolved.

| Finding | Answer | Where | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A documented test name may be stale | Every test name cited above is required to exist in the suite | `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` | `test_every_test_named_in_the_acceptance_map_exists_in_the_suite`, `test_the_map_references_enough_tests_that_this_check_is_not_vacuous` |
| A cited name must identify one test | No test name may be declared by two modules, or a row points at two different things | `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` | `test_no_test_name_is_declared_by_two_modules` |
| The map may cover only a corner of the suite | Every test module must be cited by at least one row | `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` | `test_every_test_module_in_the_suite_is_cited_by_the_map` |
| A cited source path may not exist | Every backticked artifact in the map is resolved on disk, as a file, a glob or a directory | `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` | `test_every_artifact_named_in_the_acceptance_map_resolves`, `test_the_artifact_resolver_refuses_a_path_that_does_not_exist` |
| The checker itself may be vacuous | The extractor has a positive control, a prose negative control, and a mutation control that plants a name which does not exist | `tests/test_acceptance_map.py` | `test_the_test_name_extractor_finds_names_that_are_there`, `test_the_test_name_extractor_collects_nothing_from_prose`, `test_a_cited_test_that_does_not_exist_is_reported_missing`, `test_a_cited_test_that_does_exist_is_not_reported_missing` |
| A test may be written and never run | Every test method the loader finds on a `TestCase` under `tests/` must appear in the suite that discovery builds, and the uncollected ones are named; this was measured, not imagined: a hand-listed `load_tests` hook had dropped a whole class | `tests/test_suite_integrity.py` | `test_no_test_method_is_defined_but_uncollected`, `test_nothing_collected_was_not_defined`, `test_discovery_is_not_vacuous` |
| A test may be written on a class the loader ignores | Every class under `tests/` whose body defines a `test_*` method must be collected under its own name | `tests/test_suite_integrity.py` | `test_every_class_that_writes_a_test_is_collected_under_its_name` |
| The suite checker itself may be vacuous | Positive and negative controls for both extractors, and a mutation control that builds a module with a hand-listed hook and confirms the dropped class is reported | `tests/test_suite_integrity.py` | `test_ast_extractor_finds_a_class_with_a_test_and_skips_one_without`, `test_defined_extractor_uses_the_loaders_rules`, `test_mutation_control_an_uncollected_test_is_reported` |

## Provenance of the review, and one correction to it

The review that set this design was written against a proposal, not against a
tree. One of its statements is therefore stale rather than wrong: it recorded
that the repository did not exist at review time. It does now -- this repository
is the answer to that review -- and every row above is a claim about code that
is present here and a test that runs offline.

Nothing else in the review is treated as stale. Its findings are the acceptance
criteria, including the ones this repository answers by refusing to build the
feature at all; those are listed in the closing section rather than dropped.

## What this repository deliberately does not do

Stated here because a missing feature is otherwise indistinguishable from an
oversight.

- **It does not apply anything on a schedule.** There is no code path from a
  timer to a file write, and `packaging/` prints templates rather than
  installing them.
- **It does not edit rubrics.** Not on the daily path, not on the apply path.
  Changing a rubric is a human edit to a protected file, on purpose.
- **It does not score continuous quality.** The verdict space is three discrete
  values, because a 0-to-1 score with no inter-rater reliability was one of the
  review's named risks.
- **It does not read raw tool-output logs**, which the review identified as the
  only place the missing evidence exists and simultaneously as the least
  curated, highest-sensitivity artifact on the machine. That trade is left to a
  human decision rather than made silently by a daily job.
- **It does not claim the mirror problem is solved.** It refuses to write into an
  ambiguous tree and reports the divergence. Choosing a canonical source is a
  human act.
